# Journal

> One entry per completed piece of work, newest first. What changed, what was decided, what is next. Short — the commits and ADRs hold the detail.

---

## 2026-11-22 · P9-16 ✅ · Project code in Docker unless the person chooses otherwise

- **Changed:** supervised `wl verify suite` and the new `wl exec` worker command run project commands in one warm container per slice workspace: the person's cached image, the worktree mounted at `/workspace`, the person's user ID, no network, no capabilities or privilege escalation, bounded processes, memory and CPU, and only `HOME=/tmp`. The container is reused while the slice lives and removed when its worktree stops or on `wl exec --stop`. A new `verify.suite-execution` event records with the suite intent whether a round ran in Docker (image and identifier) or on the host and who chose it; results show **Ran in** and `verify begin` shows **Project code** before anything runs.
- **Choices:** host execution is `--execution host` for one suite run or `wl setup --execution host --scope project` for the repository, displayed as not isolated; `wl setup` refuses it at user scope, and `wl exec` follows only the repository choice, so a builder cannot pick the host. The runner refuses an executor combined with a host or image choice. Without Docker, or without an image, the suite refuses before planning anything (exit 3) and names both remedies. Docker is queried only when code is about to run; only the suite, `wl exec` and worktree stop reference the container module.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0257](../docs/decisions/0257-where-project-code-runs.md) records the conservative refinements, with [ADR-0245](../docs/decisions/0245-require-docker-for-project-code-execution.md) pointing to them. Rejected: reusing the runner's acceptance-mapped executor, which needs grants and plans a supervised step lacks; a container per command; copying instead of mounting the worktree; pulling missing images. Network for dependency setup widens containment, so it stays proposal 0260 with a manual verification row; until then repositories that need a Linux install cannot run their suite in the container.
- **Also:** a first full gate caught the builder instruction naming the core command, which the role-tool invariant forbids for roles without a shell; the builder now refers to the protocol rule instead. Words after `--` now belong to the passed-through command, so `--json` or `--help` there never reach `wl`. The Docker CLI receives only `PATH`, `HOME` and its connection variables, as the runner's does. Existing verification tests choose host execution explicitly. Builders and the build and verification protocols carry the new rules for all seven clients, with enforcers in `enforcement.yml`.
- **Documentation:** the README, execution isolation, verify-works, build-slice, configuration and troubleshooting describe the default and the choice; one generated command reference was regenerated and the help recording re-rendered for the new command. Manual verification gains native builder observation, a Linux rehearsal and the proposal decision. Counts are 120/139, with five Phase 9 tasks open.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 698s`, 5336 tests in 359 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 633s`, 4140 related tests in 266 files. `test/unit/execution/execution.test.ts` passes 12 fixture cases, and its gated rehearsal against Docker Desktop 29.9.3 on macOS arm64 with `node:22-bookworm` passed: suite and worker command reported Linux, `HOME=/tmp`, a hidden host path, an absent host variable and blocked network, leaving no container behind. Required CI does not run the rehearsal; Linux rehearsal and native client observations remain manual. No model or signed-in client was used.

## 2026-11-22 · P9-15 ✅ · Worker models per tier and client

- **Changed:** a person can map each canonical tier (`fast`, `standard`, `deep`) to a model per client. Setup asks one question per tier for the executing client, with free text and the current choice named; `wl setup --models <client>:<tier>=<model>` saves it at an explicit scope. Choices are remembered answers `model:<client>:<tier>`, validated as `inherit` or a bounded model identifier; a malformed value is refused, never skipped.
- **Rendering:** `wl install` reads only the installation scope's own configuration file and writes the mapped value into the native model field of all seven clients, with a sentence naming it as configured. Unmapped output, `wl build`, marketplace plugins and maintainer renders stay byte-identical; Claude Code keeps its haiku/sonnet/opus aliases until a tier is mapped, and explicit `inherit` changes only Claude Code. Doctor compares installations with the same scoped mapping, so a changed choice reads as out of date.
- **Preview:** research, framing, verification and review previews add the configured model to each mapped tier and a "configured for" line, read only when a packaged client launched the command. Headless previews stay unmapped because headless workers receive no model.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0258](../docs/decisions/0258-proportional-depth-and-gate-cost.md) owns the refinement, with a pointer in [ADR-0048](../docs/decisions/0048-native-target-layouts-and-checked-launcher-roots.md). The criterion's "default stays inherit" holds for six clients; changing Claude Code's alias default to inherit was rejected because it would silently move verifiers to the session model, often the most expensive. Also rejected: a new key in the frozen `config@1` schema, merging user and project mappings at installation, mappings in plugin builds or headless recipes, and offering model names nobody chose. Coverage, quorums and authority are unchanged.
- **Documentation:** configuration gains a worker-models section; setup, the cost-preview guide, platforms and authoring explain the mapping. Manual verification records the native observation for all seven clients, including whether Copilot and Gemini honour the custom-agent model header. One generated reference was regenerated for the new setup option; no recorded command output changed, so no terminal GIF was regenerated. Counts are 119/139, with six Phase 9 tasks open.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 714s`, 5321 tests in 358 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 532s`, 3173 related tests in 161 files. `test/unit/workers/models.test.ts` passes 20 cases, including all seven renderers, per-scope installation and doctor, and the compiled setup command. Related adapter, installation, worker, display, gate, research and contract suites passed 1377 tests in 39 files. macOS arm64 locally; required CI supplies Linux and Node 20 checks. No native client, model account or live evaluation was used.

## 2026-11-22 · P9-14 ✅ · Structural checks of committed code citations

- **Changed:** research submission validates versioned code references against locally available committed blobs: exact file, valid line range and literal excerpt. Every failure names its claim and refuses the entire angle before any result/check event commits. Pending code jobs receive the observed local revision; successful checks travel only with their own claim to independent examiners and the derived brief.
- **Boundaries:** fixed local-only Git batches disable lazy fetching and replacement objects, never request filters/text conversion or follow symlinks, and bound commands, reference counts and unique blob bytes. Duplicate references share object reads. Actual SHA-1 and SHA-256 repositories, dirty checkouts, historical objects, configured filters and unsupported tooling are covered. Missing objects stay unavailable without a network or unguarded fallback.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0021](../docs/decisions/0021-journaled-research-plans-and-refutations.md) owns the refinement. Trusting checkout bytes, accepting only the valid part of a source result, counting quotes as semantic votes and retroactively rechecking old evidence were rejected. The new additive receipt binds exact submitted quotation hashes; replay refuses changed, duplicate or future-linked proof without opening Git. Legacy results stay unchecked. All configured independent examinations remain; the deterministic-check part of ADR-0258 removes repeated mechanical inspection, with no claimed quota or latency saving.
- **Documentation:** the research guide, canonical roles and protocol explain citation syntax, bounded failure and structural versus semantic evidence; all seven clients receive them. Four generated references remain current. No recorded command or illustrative transcript changed, so no terminal GIF was regenerated. No new task or manual evidence gap was introduced. Counts are 118/139, with seven Phase 9 tasks open; the phase table also includes the previously added P9-23 in its 23-task count.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 715s`, 5301 tests in 357 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 636s`, 3863 related tests in 259 files, followed by a 12s fast gate with all 34 citation cases. Four initial focused files passed 517 tests. Coverage includes real compiled CLI refusal/success, atomic rollback, replay, future-result linkage, legacy compatibility, constant three-command batches, byte limits, all seven renderers and retained three-slot semantic examinations. macOS arm64 locally; required CI supplies Linux and Node 20 checks. No hosted product trial, native account, live source query or model quota was used.

## 2026-11-22 · P9-13 ✅ · Cost previews before workers start

- **Changed:** research, framing challenges, verification and review expose current unfinished worker calls, requested role tiers and declared input size before dispatch. Submitted results/votes reduce the estimate; known findings add their independent examinations. Deterministic suite/testability work is excluded from model-worker counts. Later batches refresh the estimate, and framing retains one challenger at every communication style.
- **Presentation:** native instructions show the returned core display before starting workers. Shared research/gate hosts can observe a preview before each phase; an attached preview failure stops before work. Checked headless progress prints a bounded core-formatted estimate before any subprocess and retains the existing completion lines. All seven rendered clients carry the common rule and framing refresh.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0258](../docs/decisions/0258-proportional-depth-and-gate-cost.md) defines estimates in worker calls and declared payload bytes, with additional role/protocol text, read evidence and client overhead excluded. Requested tiers are not observed client models. Guessed token rates, prices or duration, counting completed work, and changing frozen plans for presentation metadata were rejected. Coverage, quorums, budgets and execution authority remain unchanged; only immutable bundled tier metadata is retained between calls.
- **Documentation:** the shared flow guide explains the preview and all four step pages link to it. Existing illustrative recordings are explicitly abridged and omit cost previews; no recorded CLI command invokes the changed plans, and their source transcripts are unchanged, so no GIF was regenerated. Four generated references remain current. Native presentation and actual quota evidence stay with P9-21 and baseline calibration; manual verification records the specific cost observation. No new task was added; counts are 117/139, with eight Phase 9 tasks open.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 697s`, 5265 tests in 356 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 416s`, 1720 tests in 115 files, then an 8s scope refinement. Three targeted files pass 86 tests, including fifteen cost cases, exact pending plan/vote counts, UTF-8 bytes, unknown prices, framing style parity, before-start ordering and all seven renderers. Actual synthetic headless subprocesses cannot appear in the process log before their preview callback; compiled progress still has one completion per worker. The first targeted run exposed the expected extra preview lines in an old completion-only assertion; the revised test verifies both ordering and all completions. macOS arm64 locally; required CI supplies Linux and Node 20 checks. No live model, signed-in client or hosted product trial ran.

## 2026-11-22 · P9-12 ✅ · Available suites and readable failure evidence

- **Changed:** known spawn failures and exit 126/127 name the declared command, executable and cause, stop before workers or a code verdict, and retain an availability receipt without spending an evaluation of the code. A corrected environment gets a distinct plan carrying only unused budget; previous failed code rounds stay spent. Workspace changes, submitted criterion work, votes, signals and ambiguous interrupted intents retain conservative failure or inspection rules. The old intent never executes again.
- **Recovery:** standalone verification reuses its pinned comparison base instead of replacing it with the worktree checkout base. The actual workflow regression exposed this mismatch; restoring the agreed base permits recovery without accepting a different comparison.
- **Output:** optional result version 2 names SHA-256 fields explicitly and puts at most 20 lines/2048 characters of transient, control-free output into live failed findings. Plain text uses this presentation; JSON without the flag and durable readers retain v1. Cached observations cannot reconstruct missing output. All seven canonical clients select v2. Only the two new contract identities were added to the compatibility inventory; every frozen declaration stays unchanged.
- **Decision and scope:** [ADR-0033](../docs/decisions/0033-once-per-round-suites-and-explicit-verification-inputs.md) records the refinement. The original criterion's wording was clarified: the planned write-ahead intent remains in history, while startup failure stops before a recorded code verdict or spent evaluation round. Erasing a real intent, resetting earlier failures, silently renaming v1 hashes and persisting even bounded process logs were rejected. No wider repair authority, new task or manual evidence gap was introduced; counts are 116/139, with nine Phase 9 tasks open.
- **Documentation:** the verification guide and execution protocol explain startup ownership, conservative exclusions, carried budgets, response versions and transient excerpts. Four generated references remain current. No existing terminal recording invokes the changed command, and normal recorded layouts remain unchanged.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 696s`, 5248 tests in 355 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 633s`, 4046 tests in 261 files. Four targeted files pass 510 tests, including real missing/non-executable commands, exit 126/127, compiled CLI text and both JSON versions, budget/intent forgery refusals, signal handling, actual workflow recovery and seven-client rendering. The initial gate caught an invalid option returning shape exit 4 instead of input exit 2; the corrected run passes. macOS arm64 locally; required CI exercises Linux and Node 20. No hosted product trial, signed-in native client, model quota or live experience evaluation ran.

## 2026-11-22 · P9-22 ✅ · Streamed step inputs and combined preparation

- **Changed:** small decisions, results and normalized observations accept explicit bounded --stdin against the same domain schemas as files. Legacy readers and a literal dash filename remain compatible. Empty, malformed, oversized or ambiguous input omits its content; payload and attribution rationale cannot both consume stdin. Source selection, ownership, current workspace checks and explicit decisions retain their guards.
- **Preparation:** research begin can return its first plan in one invocation; questions still stop before workers. Ready framing returns isolated schema copies rather than requiring another schema call, without adding them to missing-input questions. Review reuses its initial returned review/assignments; changed rounds still obtain fresh input. No cache substitutes for an authoritative check.
- **Artifacts:** the core returns one stable directory per store/run under the operating system's temporary root, physically outside repositories and the private state store. It creates no directory just to answer or return a path. Canonical instructions stream small results quietly and put remaining large artifacts there; all seven clients retain the same policy. Large ramp plans keep their file reader. A lint rejects reintroduced small-result file instructions, and tests check canonical stdin flags against the command registry.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0003](../docs/decisions/0003-deterministic-core-cli.md) records explicit input mode, limits, compound preparation and artifact ownership. Implicit dash interpretation, shared payload/rationale streams, editable artifacts in the private store, eager/shared mutable schema metadata and removing fresh checks were rejected. No task or manual acceptance gap was added; counts are 114/139, with eleven Phase 9 tasks open.
- **Documentation:** the core CLI and research guides explain streaming, limits, source questions and returned preparation. Four generated references were checked and remained current. Existing human layouts and recorded commands are unchanged, so no terminal GIF required regeneration. Synthetic experience transcripts count four bookkeeping calls before and two after; they establish no live latency, quota use or calibrated budget.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 701s`, 5199 tests in 353 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 522s`, 2852 related tests in 140 files, then `CHECK PASS (fast) in 223s`, 886 tests in 58 files. Sixteen new command cases use actual local pipes, exact final-review bytes, a real synthetic Git snapshot for gate results/votes, normalized imported observations, resumption, containment, isolated metadata and all seven generated clients. Early targeted failures exposed an incorrect review fixture, an oversize-pipe fixture that exceeded the intended limit by too much, a missing import and successful-return type narrowing; corrected tests and gates passed. macOS arm64 locally; required CI supplies Linux coverage. No live model, signed-in native client or hosted trial ran. Existing P9-20/P9-21 native evidence remains manual.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-10 ✅ · Attention-first status with actionable decisions

- **Changed:** status groups nested work by Waiting on you, Due and In progress, ranks subtrees by attention then recency and clips one row per item to terminal width. Closed rows require --all; JSON retains every selected stored item and gains parent links, direct child counts, pending-decision readiness, recorded gate results, due checkpoints, workspaces and active-child focus. The seven generated status skills forward the same plain tree.
- **Authority:** status and overview share the approval transaction's producing-step guard. A running step creates an upcoming obligation without asking for approval; the test executes the exact actionable suggestion with its explicit person, outcome and basis. A parent's displayed child never advances the parent's own flow. Recorded checks never certify current workspace bytes.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0256](../docs/decisions/0256-person-facing-display-rendered-by-the-core.md) records preserving the frozen overview schema and all selected JSON records, keeping closed ancestor context for open children, and terminal-only colour with NO_COLOR precedence. Filtering machine records, proposing premature approvals and using a child's activity as parent completion were rejected. No task or manual acceptance gap was added.
- **Cost:** one history read and one configuration observation replace repeated per-item reads; subject histories are indexed for the view while complete global merge proofs remain available. Normal state validation stays fresh. An empty store avoids unrelated configuration validation, and human text renders once. Tests bound subject-read growth at 100/400 items against the reference flow results; no latency improvement is claimed.
- **Documentation:** README, getting started and utilities show actual synthetic status output. References were regenerated and remained current. The 234214-byte help, 106640-byte core-tour and 58114-byte illustrative status GIFs were regenerated in disposable homes and visually checked.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 708s`, 5183 tests in 352 files; the initial `CHECK PASS (fast) in 270s` covered 2052 related tests in 90 files, and the empty-store refinement passed 145 tests in six files in 11s. Sixteen new status cases include compiled text/JSON/--all routes, compatibility, read-only observations and all seven rendered skills. A real local pseudo-terminal passed 40-column clipping, NO_COLOR precedence, FORCE_COLOR=0 and plain redirected FORCE_COLOR=1 output. Early targeted failures exposed an invalid fixture allocation, a recency fixture tie and trimmed tree indentation; corrected runs passed. macOS arm64 locally; required CI supplies Linux coverage. No live model, signed-in client, hosted trial or service call ran.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-09 ✅ · Human command output and explicit raw documents

- **Changed:** ordinary command handlers present structured observations directly as human text. Next, setup, resumption, configuration, profiles, overview, workspaces and catalogs have purpose-specific views; other inspection results use bounded labelled facts. The returned next step contract gains the existing display block additively, fixing its canonical skill's missing display without another invocation. Questions retain offered labels, values and free text. Open defaults to a readable document with notes/links; the additive --raw flag retains its complete projection. Raw and JSON are distinct output modes; ordinary Markdown horizontal rules remain content.
- **Diagnostics:** Doctor develops failures only and aggregates optional observations once. No failures is not a certification of unknown checks. Dry runs report no written installation record; specification problems include their supplied path and preserve exit 4.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0256](../docs/decisions/0256-person-facing-display-rendered-by-the-core.md) records direct structured presentation and explicit raw output. Parsing stdout, exposing private profile prompts and claiming that unknown checks passed were rejected. Native guard and private process wire responses stay exact; presentation grants no approval or execution authority. No task was added.
- **Documentation:** README, getting started, profile examples and the generated command reference match the new views. The 233704-byte help GIF shows its full header in a taller viewport; the 174419-byte installation GIF uses a temporary home and a version-only fixture. Both were rendered and visually checked. The installation illustration records 203 files and makes no native certification claim.

Sixteen new presentation snapshots were reviewed. ## 2026-11-21 · P9-07 ✅ · One core display for every step

- **Changed:** step commands attach wholeloop/display@1 through one shared emitter before serialization. Human output uses its exact text: one bounded header, aligned rows, observed progress, produced artifacts, open questions and the available next step. All thirteen layouts preserve decisions and unavailable evidence; profile style and coordination acknowledgements stay private.
- **Facts retained:** document failures list every actual problem; recorded builder plans return their already-validated steps/tests/assumptions without another query. Measurement unknowns, pending publication, unperformed flag changes and declared undo arguments remain visible. Parsed error routing names the requested check, ignores unrelated adapter values and leaves unknown checks neutral.
- **Progress:** a stateless formatter consumes native completion observations. Headless callbacks use the same one-line elapsed format, with --progress explicit for JSON callers; cached rounds emit no new completion. Presentation observer failure cannot invalidate evidence. Actual native forwarding, absence of raw JSON and completion timing remain open in manual verification.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0256](../docs/decisions/0256-person-facing-display-rendered-by-the-core.md) and [ADR-0010](../docs/decisions/0010-interaction-profile-never-changes-coverage.md) record verbatim phase output, private style and observation-based progress. Model-specific layouts, repeated state reads, JSON round trips and clock-based completion guesses were rejected. [ADR-0205](../docs/decisions/0205-public-controller-bundle.md) keeps the existing 4 MiB copy bound: whitespace compaction produces a 3028603-byte public core, and the build refuses overflow without changing identifiers, syntax or authority.
- **Documentation:** research begins and finishes with text from the renderer; its completion lines use the shared formatter. The 236862-byte research and 126858-byte framing illustrative GIFs were rendered locally, matched to their page transcripts and checked visually. Generated references include the formatter and remain current. No new task was added; P9-23 remains open after the original order.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 705s`, 5119 tests in 349 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 12s`, 286 related tests in 7 files. The broader fast gate passed 1796 tests in 74 files in 248s; focused suites passed 779 tests in 11 files and supplemental CLI suites passed 358 tests in four files. Twenty-one snapshots were reviewed. Earlier completed fast gates exposed missing help reachability, a 1228-byte public-core overflow and duplicate ADR headings; their replacements passed. An earlier full gate was intentionally stopped to repair the reproduced async error header and is excluded from evidence. Existing contracts stay frozen; only the display source and progress output identities were registered. macOS arm64 locally; required CI supplies Linux coverage. No native model or live source call ran.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-04 ✅ · Concurrent read-only work and complete joins

- **Changed:** pace stages decisions and writing work; independent read-only assignments launch together at every pace within host capacity. Canonical research, review, verification and measurement skills retain handles and use native joins, without fixed sleeps. Explicit sequential fallback preserves coverage.
- **Assignments:** research plans, review begin/input and suite views return self-contained pending jobs with the question, exact allowed source, observed input, response schema and final-message destination. Refuters receive original claims/findings without other votes. Cached suite views never rerun commands and mark raw output unavailable. Existing public plan, journal and compatibility schemas are unchanged.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0010](../docs/decisions/0010-interaction-profile-never-changes-coverage.md) is amended. Job-local schema refinements bind response identities without redefining public schema IDs. Serializing read-only work through guided pace, clock-based waits and copying full plans or votes into worker context were rejected. Every started job joins before an error is reported.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 678s`, 4906 tests in 346 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 409s`, 1503 related tests in 115 files. The focused suites passed 122 tests in seven files; updated profile/begin snapshots and four new assignment snapshots were reviewed. The first fast gate failed on 13 outdated begin snapshots; after the intended style-only updates, its replacement passed. Promise barriers prove overlap and complete joins without timing assertions; all seven rendered clients preserve launch-before-join policy. Generated references remain current. macOS arm64 locally; Linux remains for required CI. Native overlap and actual wait primitives remain in manual verification; no native or model call ran.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-03 ✅ · Usable named worker roles and direct startup

- **Changed:** workers return structured final messages for conductor validation/submission. Read-only roles no longer request CLI commands or result-file writes. Canonical delegating skills name the role, supply only its assignment/protocol/schema and forbid copied conductor history. An unavailable fresh native context uses an explicitly selected checked route or remains unavailable.
- **Rendering:** generation checks actual tool configuration against command/native-tool requirements and refuses incompatible output. No permission is added. Claude observers omit Bash as well as write tools; plugin permissionMode is supplementary, not the boundary. Callable names distinguish Claude's plugin scope from installed loose agents and preserve Antigravity's namespace. Role names derive from canonical files rather than a second manual list.
- **Startup:** a leading --with-context on the first packaged JSON command returns wholeloop/adapter-invocation@1 with checked locations, callable roles and the unchanged result. The command runs once; ordinary output, diagnostics, exits and the explicit root command remain compatible. The same checked declaration supplies client attribution even when role metadata is malformed.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0002](../docs/decisions/0002-open-standards-canonical-source.md) and [ADR-0047](../docs/decisions/0047-self-contained-client-artifacts-and-owned-output-upgrades.md) are amended. Wider worker tools, copied history, automatic client-setting changes and wrapping existing outputs by default were rejected. Only two new schema/output fingerprints are registered; every existing compatibility fingerprint is unchanged.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 678s`, 4887 tests in 344 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 479s`, 2243 related tests in 119 files. The focused suites passed 817 tests in 14 files, including all seven full installed catalogs, plugin versus loose-agent names, incompatible roles, writers without shell and malformed role metadata retaining client identity. The final role-tool suite passed 36 cases, with overlapping coverage. Seven adapter goldens were reviewed and updated. An earlier fast gate was interrupted to strengthen exact-tool checking; its replacement passed. Generated references remained current. macOS arm64 locally; Linux remains for required CI. Native loading and actual context isolation remain in manual verification. No native or model call ran.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-02 ✅ · Explicit personal guidance and run preferences

- **Changed:** description-only matches are excluded; personal names match complete method aliases or explicit tags and remain optional offers. Wholeloop’s default is first, equivalent content is deduplicated and shared physical folders are walked once. Repository guidance remains automatic within its declared precedence.
- **Client scope:** personal discovery uses the executing client or an explicit client choice, including native directory overrides. Unknown clients scan no personal skills. Other-client locations are listed without loading their files until requested; the repository scan still covers every client.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0009](../docs/decisions/0009-repo-first-capability-resolution.md) is amended. Run choices are versioned external preference configuration, not a reinterpretation of frozen journal events. Anonymous questions return a run ID for the answer and repeated begin; existing run identities cannot be reassigned. Every answer validates the offered value before writing and names its destination.
- **Consumers:** build, review, verification and Rollout/Learn guidance read the selected run’s preferences. An explicit default remains the built-in method. Remembered personal content is readable only inside a declared skill location, never by granting arbitrary home-file access.
- **Regression found:** the first affected-test gate exposed an autonomous planner that appended its own default after the resolver’s options. It now preserves the resolver’s single, default-first list; configured defaults also bypass file pinning. The 13 autonomous guidance tests passed after the correction.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 679s`, 4834 tests in 342 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 617s`. All 444 compatibility cases passed. The focused suites passed 277 affected-workflow tests in 22 files, 19 personal-guidance cases and 13 autonomous guidance tests, with overlapping coverage. Generated references were current. Renaming a discovery test also required removing its obsolete snapshot key; its expected contents were unchanged. macOS arm64 locally; Linux and actual native policies remain for required CI and the existing manual checks. The experience baseline instructions now name the pre-fix P9-20 revision explicitly.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-01 ✅ · Recoverable external-store permission failures

- **Changed:** store `EACCES` and `EPERM` failures now return exit 3 with the resolved path and a targeted hint for the declared client. Both CLI dispatchers preserve that diagnosis when handlers wrap filesystem errors. All seven clients have bounded recovery wording.
- **Codex:** install adds a review-only configuration snippet to human and JSON output; doctor checks declared user/project writable roots separately from its actual write probe. Missing or unreadable settings stay unknown, and active overrides may still refuse access. No client settings are changed.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0004](../docs/decisions/0004-state-outside-the-repo.md) is amended. Automatic writable-root changes and putting state inside the checkout were rejected; the person chooses the directory allowance. Troubleshooting includes observed exit-3 output from a disposable home.
- **Compatibility:** the first full gate found three failures after an optional field definition changed the frozen install declaration. The declaration is restored unchanged; the additive payload metadata remains valid under its existing permissive contract. No baseline fingerprint is refreshed.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 773s`, 4815 tests in 341 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 102s` for the initial change and `CHECK PASS (fast) in 47s` after restoring the frozen declaration. All 444 compatibility cases passed. The 45 focused permission and existing doctor tests passed, including human/JSON CLI refusals, both permission error codes, seven client hints, configuration precedence and installation without configuration writes. macOS arm64 locally; Linux remains for required CI. Unix mode-bit refusal cases skip for root, which bypasses those bits.

## 2026-11-21 · P9-20 ✅ · Observed experience budgets

- **Changed:** a six-step external fixture evaluation for Codex and Claude Code, native transcript parsers, worker overlap and visible-output measurements, absolute budgets, offline replay and baseline calibration. The entry point refuses live work before launch without explicit quota confirmation; no live experience run was made.
- **Decision:** [ADR-0259](../docs/decisions/0259-observed-experience-budgets.md) keeps unreported usage, incomplete worker observations and missing live calibration explicit. Synthetic transcripts test the arithmetic and never establish a live baseline. Token, first-question, call-count and build-gate ceilings come from twelve live step traces and can only tighten. Arbitrary initial ceilings and automatic permission grants were rejected.
- **Acceptance:** locally complete with live execution, the pre-fix reference-client baseline and calibration left in [manual verification](./manual-verification.md#p9-20-baseline-capture). That baseline must use this task's commit before later fixes. Native streams may omit nested activity; the per-client pass checks their limits.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 751s`, 4792 tests in 340 files; `CHECK PASS (fast) in 9s` for the final measurement corrections. All 23 focused parser, provenance, budget and refusal tests passed. Offline synthetic replay failed exactly on missing live calibration, as intended. macOS arm64 only; Linux execution remains for CI. The sandboxed repository suite was interrupted after an existing runner lease test failed; the same test passed with process access outside the sandbox.

## 2026-11-21 · Decision · Proportional depth and gate cost (ADR-0258)

- **Decision:** [ADR-0258](../docs/decisions/0258-proportional-depth-and-gate-cost.md) is accepted in full and amends ADR-0010. Cost first falls without touching coverage (deterministic citation checks, cost shown before every fan-out, parallel read-only workers, a cheaper verifier model when mapped, applicability of optional lenses); findings carry `blocker`, `should` or `nit` and only blockers fail a round; and research and gates take an explicit rigor of `quick`, `standard` or `deep`, proposed by the step and confirmed by the person, never set by the profile. `standard` keeps today's coverage, reduced coverage is recorded and shown, and nothing drops below the refutation quorum.
- **Naming:** the setting is rigor, not depth, because the profile's `depth` dial already sets vocabulary and level of detail.
- **Why:** without explicit rigor, any research question costs one worker per source plus three verifiers per load-bearing claim; a question answerable from the repository in a few minutes should cost about one worker.
- **Next:** no decision is open in [Phase 9](./phase-9-user-experience.md) except P9-18's implement-or-remove choices; the order starts with the baseline (P9-20) and P9-01–P9-05.

---

## 2026-11-21 · Decision · Person-facing display rendered by the core (ADR-0256)

- **Decision:** [ADR-0256](../docs/decisions/0256-person-facing-display-rendered-by-the-core.md) is accepted. Every step command's payload gains a versioned `display` block and its text at 80 columns; the same renderer produces the CLI's human output; skills show `display.text` verbatim, add only what the person needs to decide and never narrate internal fields or raw JSON. ADR-0010's output contract still holds, with the preflight and receipt shown as the header and produced lines; while workers run, only one progress line per finished worker is shown.
- **Why:** each model improvised the presentation from payloads full of internal fields, which was most of the noise in the 2026-11-17 trial; presentation has one right answer per payload, so it belongs in the core with snapshot tests.
- **Next:** P9-07 is no longer waiting on a decision; P9-08 and P9-09 follow it. Proposal 0258 is the only open decision in [Phase 9](./phase-9-user-experience.md).

---

## 2026-11-21 · Decision · Where project code runs (ADR-0257)

- **Decision:** [ADR-0257](../docs/decisions/0257-where-project-code-runs.md) is accepted and amends ADR-0245. Every execution of project code runs in Docker by default, supervised or autonomous, including the tests and scripts builder and verifier workers run, through a `wl` command in a per-slice environment kept warm. Steps that only read (research, framing, specification, scopes, slicing, review, Rollout and Learn) never check for Docker. A person may choose host execution for a supervised run or repository; it is shown before the step, recorded and displayed as not isolated. The autonomous runner never allows it.
- **Why:** the supervised path ran project code on the host while the README promised Docker; reading steps should not pay for isolation they do not need.
- **Found while deciding:** builder and verifier workers also run tests through the client's own shell, outside `wl`. P9-16 grows to cover them and now depends on P9-03; [Execution isolation](../docs/architecture/execution-isolation.md) lists both as known gaps until it lands.
- **Next:** unchanged; P9-16 follows the order of [Phase 9](./phase-9-user-experience.md#order).

---

## 2026-11-18 · Roadmap · Phase 9, user experience for 1.2.0

- **Why:** a first trial of `wl-research` in Codex 0.167.0, on a question about this repository's own code, took 14.6 minutes and about 9.3 million input tokens across nine workers, queried PostgreSQL and Notion without asking, and narrated internal terms to the person. The claims were correct; the experience was not ready to announce.
- **Causes found:** the client's run logs, the code and a walk through every step in an isolated store located them. Workers ran one after another because the default profile says "Run one worker at a time", with 441 of 875 seconds spent in fixed 45-second sleeps. Codex read-only roles have no command tool but are told to run `wl`, so full-context copies of the conductor replaced them, spent the tokens and ran the repository's tests on the host. Every detected source becomes a research angle, including an executable on `PATH` and MCP servers declared for another client. Guidance discovery matches personal skills by one word of their description and blocks `build-slice` on that question. A metric's provider is free text first checked after launch. No display layout is defined and most commands print JSON without `--json`. Gates use three verifiers per finding as a schema floor, all six review lenses on any change, and fail a round on any finding.
- **Guarantee gap:** supervised `wl verify suite` runs the project's suite on the host with the caller's environment, while the README and ADR-0245 say project code runs only in Docker; a fixture suite reported `platform=darwin` with Docker running. Proposal 0257 asks which of code or claim changes.
- **Plan:** [Phase 9](./phase-9-user-experience.md) lists 22 tasks and stays open for more; 1.2.0 becomes the user-experience release and the factory phases move to 1.3.0–1.5.0 (ADR-0247 amended). Proposals 0256 (display rendered by the core), 0257 (project code in supervised verification) and 0258 (proportional depth and gate cost, against ADR-0010) wait for the maintainer.
- **Next:** a recorded baseline of the current behaviour (P9-20), then P9-01–P9-05, which together address the trial's time, tokens and unasked sources.

---

## 2026-11-17 · Docs · Readability pass before announcing the package

- **Why:** the style guide asked for "concise and dense" pages with reasons linked to ADRs, and the pages followed it: 138 prose paragraphs over 80 words, 552 ADR references outside the decision records, and 3 of 106 terminal examples with their output. ADR-0016 is amended with the new rules: short paragraphs, terms defined on first use, real output after every command, diagrams where an idea has moving parts, and no ADR links on pages read to use wholeloop.
- **What changed:** the README tells one story (problem, how it works, the loop, a five-step quick start); the first-use, step, configuration, client setup and autonomous delivery pages are rewritten; the step pages put the core protocol after their usage; reference pages have their longest paragraphs split at sentence boundaries; `nav.yml` is grouped by purpose. Now 4 prose paragraphs exceed 80 words and the remaining 222 ADR references sit on architecture, contributing and reference pages.
- **Real output:** every shown output was printed by wholeloop 1.1.0 in a disposable home, or by the tutorial scripts run in sequence after planning. The Docker evaluations quoted in the guides passed on macOS arm64 with Docker 29.9.3: `eval:environments`, `eval:behaviors`, `eval:browsers`, `eval:private` and `eval:assembly` with `--retire-success`, plus `eval:integration`, `eval:delivery-fixtures` and `eval:decisions`.
- **Recordings:** VHS tapes under `docs/assets/terminal` render three real `wl` recordings and sixteen illustrative step sessions, which replay each page's example session and say *Illustrative* in the recording and its caption. Recording real client sessions was rejected: they take minutes, differ on every run and spend an account's quota. Three step pages (ramp-exposure, measure-impact, decide-next) gained an example session.
- **Corrected claims:** `next` has no `--through` option; `setup` neither installs clients nor edits MCP settings; `profile show` does not report value origins; `mcp add` prints a snippet and never writes client configuration; unattended execution is no longer described as later work; a unit commits its candidate before verification and review and ends with reverification.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 693s`, 4769 tests in 339 files, on macOS arm64; Linux was not run for this docs-only change.
- **Not enforced yet:** paragraph length and the absence of ADR links on user pages are guidance; an enforcer in `wl docs check` is a separate change. markdownlint now allows `details` and `summary`.

## 2026-11-17 · P8-01 ✅ · Live CI correction attempts and the correction's inputs

- **Live scenario:** `npm run eval:live-ci-correction` runs EVAL-09's task with Codex 0.166.0 and `gpt-6.1-sol` on the trial repository's `ci-trial` branch, whose required check also runs a CI-only `ci-check.cjs`. The trial repository is public since 2026-11-16, so its Actions runs are free. Seven attempts observed a real required-check failure on GitHub, the unattended continuation under the same envelope grant, an approved correction plan and a builder producing the right repair, but none reached a green check.
- **Core gaps found live, fixed with tests:**
  - A CI correction's build plan was judged without the failed checks or the published candidate's changes; Codex workers have no read tool. The build-plan inputs of a CI correction now carry the failed checks, published head and pull request, and every changed path with its bytes, with no check output (ADR-0213).
  - The correction's builders patched stale lines from the original source; they now receive the same inputs.
  - The run's builder restarts, like the grant's effect limit, are shared with the original attempt and must be sized for repairs (ADR-0248 amended).
- **Scenario, not core:** later attempts were refused correctly. The correction's planner refused because the pinned acceptance allowed only `status.txt`; the acceptance producer refused because a release note naming the pull request, or a passing required check, cannot be verified before publication. Manual verification records each attempt and what a passing scenario needs.
- **Closed as narrowed:** P8-01's criteria are met by the unit tests and the model-free rehearsal with real Git and Docker. The live scenario stays open in manual verification, as AGENTS.md prescribes for hosted acceptance. The rehearsal now also asserts the CI facts in the correction's build-plan inputs.
- **P6-35:** Claude Code 2.1.326 takes a credential only from `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or an `apiKeyHelper`, so neither reference client has a per-launch stdin path. The calls that need the maintainer's Claude and OpenAI API credentials, the token lifetime and the live window exhaustion stay open.

## 2026-11-17 · P8-01 🔄 · CI correction continuation under the envelope grant

- **Decision:** the maintainer accepted ADR-0247 (factory phasing) and ADR-0248 (this continuation) on 2026-11-16. 1.1.0 carries this capability with the command-line work already on `main`.
- **Controller:** `runWithRepairs` now continues a `none` run after a failed required check, under the run's own still-active envelope grant. It needs `git.push` besides the repair effects, a failure classified `repair` with push budget left, and exactly one code unit, the same narrowing as P6-41. Selection reads only; acceptance records the continuation as `envelope-grant-continuation` and runs it in the same invocation.
- **Mark:** kept in the continuation receipt, because the version-one registration schema is frozen. `DeliveryLifecycle.track` admits the envelope grant on a CI correction only with that mark and `git.push`, so an operator's CI proposal still needs a new grant. A mutation that drops the check fails the operator-proposal test.
- **Findings from the model-free rehearsal** (`npm run eval:ci-continuation -- --unattended`): the continuation's verification could not run beside the failed request's live environments, and the envelope grant's effect limit is shared with the original attempt. The controller now asks the host to stop the predecessor's environments, keeping their data and evidence, before recording the continuation, and continues nothing if a stop is not observed; without that step the rehearsal fails, so it is required. The effect limit is documented: size the envelope grant for the original attempt plus its repairs. Both also apply to P6-41's continuations, whose tests were synthetic.
- **Verified:** `CHECK PASS in 670s`; five new continuation tests and two release-ordering tests; the unattended rehearsal ends with the original CI failed, the correction passed, the second CI passed, the original pull request updated and merge blocked as ungranted.
- **Open:** the live scenario with Codex on `dreamingechoes/wholeloop-trials` (branch `ci-trial`, a CI-only release-note check under branch protection).

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · README diagrams that npm can show

- **Why:** npm renders a package README without Mermaid, so the loop diagram showed as code on the package page. The README also had only that one diagram, which explains the flow but not how the pieces fit or what a step does.
- **Change:** the README's diagrams are SVG files rendered from `.mmd` sources in `docs/assets/diagrams/` by `npm run docs:diagrams` (mermaid-cli 12.2.0 through `npm exec` with a cooldown date, and the local Chrome), linked by absolute raw URL so npm can load them. Two new diagrams join the loop: how the client, skills, core, store, repository, Docker and optional services fit together, and the exchange every step has with the core.
- **Rendering choices:** plain SVG text instead of HTML labels, because images shown through `<img>` do not render HTML everywhere; a common font, because Chrome measured the boxes with the font it had; one light render on white, because a dark render made the subgraph fills unreadable and npm shows a single image.
- **Check:** each SVG starts with its source's SHA-256, and the Mermaid check parses the sources and fails on a missing or stale SVG, so the gate needs no browser.
- **Also:** the README's status line gave way to badges for the npm version, CI, Node, licence and OpenSSF Scorecard. Docs style, the development guide and ADR-0016 describe the new flow.
- **Rejected:** rewriting a separate README for the package at release time (publishes bytes that differ from the reviewed source) and a third-party Mermaid rendering service (the README would depend on it).

---

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · Status notes only where the status matters

- **Why:** after 1.0.0, 42 of the 73 status banners said a page was implemented, which a reader assumes of released documentation. They added a line of noise to most pages and hid the notes that matter.
- **Rule (ADR-0016 amended, docs style):** a page describes released behaviour unless a note right after its TL;DR says otherwise: **Experimental** (26 pages, as an `[!IMPORTANT]` alert), **Partial** (5 pages, naming what is missing) or **Design**. `wl docs check` no longer requires a banner and refuses the retired `**Status:**` form.
- **Pages:** each implemented banner was read before it went. Limits, decision links and where evidence lives stay as an ordinary sentence under the TL;DR, such as remote publication being experimental on a step page; claims that only said a feature ships or is tested were dropped. The releasing guide's banner still said the release app had not opened a pull request; it went with the rest.
- **Also:** AGENTS.md, CONTRIBUTING, the pull request template, the roadmap guide and the maintainer close-task skill and docs-auditor agent use the new rule.

---

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · npm from an exact Node in release workflows

- **Why:** four OpenSSF Scorecard `PinnedDependencies` alerts, all the same line: `npm install --global npm@11.8.0` in the release prepare and publish jobs, the release pull request job and the conformance job. A global install is not pinned by hash.
- **Change:** those jobs set up Node 24.22.0 exactly and use its bundled npm 11.19.1, newer than 11.8.0 and enough for trusted publishing. A first step fails unless `npm --version` is exactly 11.19.1. ADR-0069 and the releasing guide are amended; a test fails if a workflow installs npm globally again or a checked job leaves Node unpinned.
- **Evidence:** the package built with Node 24.22.0 and npm 11.19.1 on Linux arm64 already matched the macOS build byte for byte (manual verification). The first hosted run of this pair in publication is the 1.0.1 release.
- **Trade-off:** Dependabot does not update `node-version`, so raising Node in these jobs is a deliberate change to them.
- **Rejected:** a separate lockfile for npm 11.8.0 installed with `npm ci` (more to maintain for one binary) and dismissing the alerts (leaves an unpinned install in the publishing job).

---

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · Files are checked and read through one descriptor

- **Why (#34):** CodeQL reported 40 `js/file-system-race` alerts in `src/` and `scripts/`. Most checked a path (a regular file, not a link, within a size limit) and then read it by name, so the file read was not guaranteed to be the file checked.
- **Helper:** `src/fs/regular-file.ts` opens the path once, without following a final link unless asked, checks type and size on the descriptor and reads it to the end, refusing a file that grows past the limit. It reports why a read was refused (missing, not regular, too large, unreadable), because some callers treat those differently. `scripts/regular-file.mjs` mirrors it for the scripts, which run without a build.
- **Callers:** every flagged site now reads through it. Callers that refused links still do; the few that followed them (research reports, gate corpora, discovery inside its boundary, recovery bundles) still do, and now read what they checked. Content and mode come from the same descriptor wherever both were checked. Discovery still skips files over 1 MiB with a warning; a gate snapshot still treats a vanished file as absent.
- **Behaviour change:** a missing file at these sites now fails with the caller's own message instead of a raw `ENOENT`, and the delivery journal directory is opened directly, treating `ENOENT` as an empty journal, rather than checked for existence first.
- **Not a race:** the delivery journal segment reader already compared the opened descriptor with the path before and after reading; it carries a comment saying so, and changes nothing else.
- **CodeQL:** 39 of the 40 alerts closed with the merge. The journal segment reader's alert stayed open, because a pull request's analysis only reports the lines it changes; it was dismissed as a false positive with the reason above.
- **Tests:** the helper is tested against a link, a directory, a missing path, an oversized file, a path replaced by a link or renamed over after the open, and a file that grows after it.

---

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · Install results that point at real things

- **Backups (#32):** install and uninstall reserved a backup path before archiving anything and always reported it, so a first install named a directory that did not exist. The result now includes `backup` only when that directory was written; an upgrade and an uninstall, which keeps the removal core, still report theirs. The field was already optional in the contract.
- **Git guard command (#33):** the activation and removal commands named the directory the install ran in as the repository to guard. For a user-scope installation that directory is arbitrary, and from inside the wholeloop checkout it pointed at a repository with its own hooks. User scope now shows `<repository>` for the person to replace; project scope still names its own repository. Getting started and local client setup say so.
- **Rejected:** dropping the command at user scope. People still need to know how to add the guards, and the placeholder keeps the command copyable.

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · Readable output for the first commands

- **Why:** without `--json`, `doctor`, `status`, `install` and `uninstall` printed their JSON payload indented, so a first `wl doctor` was forty lines to read through (#41). Doctor also called an outdated but untouched installation "stale" and told the person to "preserve edits", which suggested a hand change (#31).
- **Summaries:** those four commands now print a short summary by default: doctor one line per check, with the reason and the repair command for anything that is not a pass; status one block per item with its next step and pending decisions; install and uninstall the outcome, what a dry run would change, backups, the receipt and the next step. Repair commands are shell-quoted so a store path with spaces can be copied. `--json` returns the payloads exactly as before, and every other command keeps its indented JSON.
- **Stale installations:** the installation inspection now records the version that wrote a stale installation. Doctor says which version wrote it, or that it is a different build of the same version, that none of its files were edited and that the install command updates it, keeping the replaced files as backups. Edited installations keep their existing message and dry-run repair.
- **Docs:** getting started, troubleshooting, local client setup and the README run these commands without `--json`, and getting started shows a doctor summary.

---

## 2026-11-16 · 1.0.1 · Help that starts with the everyday commands

- **Why:** `wl --help` printed 261 commands in table order, experimental delivery first; the commands a new user needs started at line 216. `wl <command> --help` printed the same page, and a typo only pointed back to it (#39, #40, #42).
- **Default help:** the commands to get started, to follow your work and to set preferences, then every other group by area. A test fails when a new group has no area, so no command becomes unreachable. `wl --help --all` prints the whole table.
- **Command and group help:** `wl <command> --help` prints its usage, options and the commands that share its group; `wl <group> --help` lists the group. An unknown command with `--help` now fails as unknown instead of printing the general page.
- **Suggestions:** a mistyped command names the closest known one, within its group when the group is right: `instal` suggests `install`, `delivery runner-sttus` suggests `delivery runner-status`. Short words allow one edit and longer ones two, so unrelated short commands are not proposed. The exit code is unchanged.
- **Kept:** `--help --json` still returns the complete `wholeloop/help@1` table wherever it appears, because scripts and the generated reference read it.

---

## 2026-11-16 · Docs · First-use pass after 1.0.0

- **Why:** a read-through as a new user found the first pages hard to follow. The docs index opened with autonomous delivery, 21 of the 25 tutorials are experimental delivery pages and most were listed among the everyday ones, and getting started and troubleshooting were dense with compressed phrasing and caveats.
- **Docs index:** everyday tutorials come first. Autonomous delivery has its own section, marked experimental, with its tutorials in the order of a run; the browser and private acceptance tutorials were missing from the index and are listed now. The navigation footers follow the same order.
- **Getting started and troubleshooting:** rewritten in plain language with the same headings, so existing links keep working. What installation does not do is gathered in one section, and the delivery problems in troubleshooting sit under their own heading.
- **Glossary:** adds install scope, owned file, step contract and target, and says that an install scope is not a scope of work.
- **Help and discovery:** Discussions is enabled for questions and linked from the README, CONTRIBUTING, the issue chooser and the docs. The package lists npm keywords that match the repository topics.
- **Not changed:** the CHANGELOG is generated from commits and checked, so a hand-written highlights section was left out. The CLI findings from the same read-through are #39 to #42 in the 1.0.1 milestone.

## 2026-11-15 · P7-05 follow-up · Code scanning triage after 1.0.0

- **Found:** 79 open code-scanning alerts after the repository went public.
- **Scope:** CodeQL no longer analyses `test/`. The 26 alerts there came from tests that build fake scripts, fixtures and deliberately unsafe inputs, none of which ship; `src/` and `scripts/` stay in scope.
- **Fixed:** `check-dependencies.mjs` escapes every `@` in a package URL. Only one can occur, but the code now says so plainly.
- **Dismissed with a reason:**
  - CodeQL #78 and #79 in conformance provisioning are by design. Downloads come only from a fixed host allowlist, refuse redirects, and are checked against the pinned integrity when one exists.
- **Scorecard exceptions:**
  - `CodeReview` and `BranchProtection` are dismissed: approvals need a second maintainer. `main` still requires pull requests, three checks, linear history and no force pushes.
  - `Fuzzing` and `CII Best Practices` are dismissed for now.
  - `Maintained` and `Vulnerabilities` will clear on their own, with the repository's age and the pending `hono` updates.
  - `PinnedDependencies`, for the global npm install in the workflows, waits for a decision.
- **Open:** the 40 `js/file-system-race` alerts in `src/` and `scripts/` are real check-then-use pairs with low risk for a local CLI. Their refactor through the opened descriptor is #34, in the 1.0.1 milestone.

## 2026-11-15 · P7-15 · Stable release notes cover the whole stable range

- **Problem:** the notes, version check and changelog section of a version started at the previous tag. For `1.0.0` that meant `1.0.0-rc.1`, so the first stable release would have listed a single documentation entry.
- **Changed:** a final version now starts at the previous final version, and a pre-release still starts at the version just before it. One function in the changelog generator holds the rule, and the release workflow uses it too. `1.0.0` therefore covers the whole history, and `1.1.0` will cover everything since `1.0.0`. ADR-0068 is amended.
- **Rejected:** keeping the previous-tag range and pointing readers to the candidate's notes.
- **Verified:** the `1.0.0` notes over the whole history are a draft with no rejections. Range tests cover candidates and final versions, and the changelog fixture checks that the final section restates its candidate's changes.

## 2026-11-15 · P7-15 · Squash-merge suffix in commit subjects

- **Problem:** previewing the `1.0.0` notes rejected three commits on `main` as non-conventional, and the release pull request's version step requires a clean draft, so it would have stopped. Their 68–69 character titles had passed every check, but GitHub's squash merge appends `(#N)`, which took them to 74–75 characters.
- **Changed:** the shared subject validator does not count a trailing `(#N)` toward the 72-character limit. The hook, the CI check and the release notes stay in agreement. ADR-0017 is amended.
- **Rejected:** requiring titles of 66 characters or fewer, which cannot repair commits already on `main`. Rewriting history is also rejected.
- **Verified:** `wl release notes --from v1.0.0-rc.1 --version 1.0.0` now returns a draft with no rejections. The new commit and notes tests fail without the change.

---

## 2026-11-15 · P7-14 follow-up · Release pull request checks without approval

- **Problem:** in the `1.0.0-rc.1` release, the CI run dispatched on the release branch never counted toward the required checks. The pull request's own runs waited in `action_required` until approved, because the workflow token had opened it.
- **Changed:** the dispatch and the job's `actions: write` are removed. When the `release-pull-request` environment configures a GitHub App, the workflow opens the pull request with a short-lived installation token instead, so its checks start on their own. The token is minted after the full gate, with only contents and pull-requests write, and revoked when the job ends. Without the app, the workflow token is used as before, and the pull request says its runs need approval.
- **Decided:** ADR-0069 is amended. Its earlier rejection of a GitHub App is reversed only as this opt-in, and a personal access token stays rejected.
- **Verified:** the workflow and fixture tests cover both token modes and the absence of a dispatch.
- **Open:** creating the app and the environment is the maintainer's setup. The behaviour on GitHub is in manual verification.

- **Switch:** changes now reach `main` only through squash-merged pull requests with passing required checks, and the bootstrap workflow of committing straight to `main` ends here. `AGENTS.md`, the contributing guide and the roadmap's working loop say so.

## 2026-11-14 · P6-27 · Codex EVAL-09 three-passes

- **Fixture version 2:** `status.txt` holds `pending` with a newline, and the app and suite ignore exactly one trailing newline. Codex's only builder tool always writes one. The maintainer merged it in `wholeloop-trials` and restored branch protection, which reads back unchanged.
- **Thirteen harness fixes**, each from a live attempt that stopped one stage later than the last. Each closed a gap between what the core enforces and what a native worker is shown:
  - expected values are JSON text, and assertion paths are JSON Pointers;
  - schema patterns and minimums travel as descriptions;
  - guidance pins name their roles;
  - builders know the controller runs every check;
  - verify and review workers get the suite result and the changed bytes;
  - verifiers defer behavior that acceptance observes, by the maintainer's choice;
  - producers get the milestone procedure and the approval rubric;
  - the acceptance judge sees the pinned assertions and the integrated changed bytes.
- **EVAL-09 on `gpt-6.1-sol`: `three-passes`** on core `c45baa1`. The trials took 651, 327 and 321 s, with no human wait, every launch host-observed and a ready pull request each (#2 to #4 in `wholeloop-trials`, left open for inspection). Together with EVAL-10, Codex meets P6-27's first criterion. Claude Code's EVAL-09 trials remain.
- **Rehearsals:** two model-free rehearsals failed by time during a Time Machine backup that saturated the disk, and passed again in 176 s afterwards.
- **Next:** Claude Code EVAL-09, then the native P6-41 repair and the P6-40 guidance trials.

The six boundary controls pass on that binary.

They came after one fix to the trial: its plan judge had been given claim-citation rules ([ADR-0206](../docs/decisions/0206-live-research-pilot-findings.md#plan-scoped-rubric-rules)).
- **EVAL-09 implementation:** the first live attempt failed at acceptance definition. `gpt-6-luna` left one quote unescaped in a runner input nested two strings deep, and the core rejected the artifact. Nothing was published. Moving EVAL-09 to a stronger model is left to the maintainer.

## 2026-11-11 · P5-01 · Gemini 0.67 read-only boundary through its policy engine

- Unpaused by the maintainer. Gemini 0.67 skips a system settings file that root does not own, and it drops project configuration in an untrusted workspace. That left the observer without its restrictions and the framing fixture without its skills.
- The observer now gets:
  - a disposable `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` whose user settings hold the same restrictions and only the user's auth method;
  - an `--admin-policy` file that denies every tool and MCP server and allows only reading;
  - `--skip-trust` for the directory wholeloop created.

  A system policy directory holding a `.toml` file would silently void a supplemental admin policy, so it now counts as an administrator policy and the worker refuses to start.
- The framing fixture gets an admin policy, written by `prepare`, that grants the exact launcher through `commandPrefix` and exact writes to the draft, decision and review files. The prompt now names those files and asks for the review in-turn, as it already did for Copilot and Antigravity. The session-label tool `update_topic` is allowed, because each denial cost a request.
- Found and rejected:
  - a hand-written `commandRegex`, which Gemini refuses as a ReDoS risk and never anchors;
  - the earlier guess that the failures came from the environment. Exit 173 is Gemini's `TerminalQuotaError`.
- **Live on 0.67.0:**
  - the read-only probe passes;
  - framing reached `frame finalize` with every grant honoured, then the free tier's daily quota (20 requests per model) ended it.

  The default model returned 503, so runs used `GEMINI_MODEL`. P5-01 stays open until a run with quota passes. ADR-0071 amended.
- Gate: CHECK PASS in 789s, 4701 tests, macOS arm64; the Linux matrix is recorded separately.

## 2026-11-08 · P6-41 ✅ · A none run repairs itself under its own envelope grant

- **Reducer:** on a correction revision it now accepts the run's own envelope grant (the one the run already ran under, still active) when the policy is `none`.
- **Repair effects:** `DeliveryLifecycle.track`, the only writer of grant events, requires `code.write`, `git.commit` and `test.run`. The revision stays inside the envelope, and the repair budget is still enforced at registration.
- **Controller:** `DeliveryRunner.runWithRepairs`, used by the foreground controller, records the correction itself (authority `envelope-grant-continuation`), attaches the grant and runs the successor in the same invocation, until the route passes or the budget ends the run.
- Narrowed to plans with exactly one code unit, because the owning unit is never guessed from failure text; other plans keep the operator path. ADR-0198 and ADR-0197 are amended.
- **Tests:** a seeded integration failure is repaired with no human step; a missing effect, a spent budget and a revoked grant are each refused. Removing the reducer exception fails the unattended cases. Delegated decision records are stubbed in these tests, and the native repair-loop trial stays with P6-27.
- Gate: CHECK PASS in 721s, 4697 tests, macOS arm64 only; Linux not run.

## 2026-11-08 · P6-35 ⏸️ · Worker credentials built; live acceptance pending

- Built in four parts:
  - **Credential references:** a runtime client can name a secret-store item (Keychain or Secret Service) instead of a signed-in home. `wl delivery credential-store|credential-check` move the value only through stdin and never print it.
  - **Injection per launch:** each launch receives its credential with a disposable client home, and Claude's sandbox profile then grants no Keychain access.
  - **Credential preflight:** it ends a run before dispatch with a named reason.
  - **Usage-window wait:** before each stage, the controller reads the usage window of the signed-in clients the stage would launch. An exhausted Codex window makes the run wait in `waiting-external`; after the reset the same run continues.
- Found: Claude reports any injected token as signed in, and `codex login status` ignores `CODEX_API_KEY`, so a credential's validity shows only at its first launch. Claude Code reports no usage window. ADR-0209 amended.
- Decided with the maintainer: the window signal is Codex's no-model `account/rateLimits/read`, read before dispatch. Rejected: pausing the run while the controller waits, which ends the controller's lease, and reading the signal from failed launches, which no client reports reliably.
- Live without a model: a Keychain check, the real Claude 2.1.319 under the injected profile, and the maintainer's Codex window (available at 96 percent). The three acceptance criteria need the maintainer's accounts and stay in manual verification.
- Gate: CHECK PASS in 675s, 4694 tests, macOS arm64 only; Linux not run.

- One disposable fixture per client: a marker skill in each discovery directory, a reader subagent, logging hooks and a local MCP server returning a fixed marker. Each client got one headless run, plus an interactive tmux session for the question tool.

## 2026-11-08 · P1-11 ✅ · Read-only roles hold on Codex, OpenCode and Copilot

- Asked the generated reviewer to overwrite a sentinel, create a file and run `touch` with each client's broadest approval switched on. Copilot names plugin agents `wholeloop:wl-<name>` and always adds `skill` and its session `sql` tool; a connected user MCP server was not reachable.

## 2026-11-08 · P4-01 ✅ · The launcher cannot reach a sending tool on any client

- Checked the two clients whose custom agents could inherit user MCP servers, with a disposable MCP server whose `send_message` records every call and a positive control from the main session. Cursor's readonly launcher saw the servers but the client refused every call. Codex's launcher received every user MCP server plus `apply_patch` and `exec`; only its instructions kept it from sending.
- Decided: Codex renders no native launcher (adapters gain `omitAgents`), and `wl launch draft --for <id> --engine headless --adapter <client>` runs the canonical launcher role in one isolated worker and stores the kit through the same validated completion. Rejected: keeping the native agent and relying on Codex's MCP approval, which depends on user settings. ADR-0038 amended.
- Found while verifying: the Codex headless recipe, also used by gate workers, still exposed ChatGPT account apps (Notion and Calendar connectors) with user config ignored and no MCP servers. It now disables apps, plugins, browser use and computer use; a live probe then listed no MCP tool. ADR-0046 amended.
- Live: `wl launch draft` on Codex 0.166 produced a validated kit; every comms entry is an unsent draft and the dashboard links its stored metric.
- Gate: CHECK PASS in 674s, 4650 tests, macOS arm64 only; Linux not run.

## 2026-11-08 · P3-08 ✅ · Guards read real payloads from every tier-A client

- Captured Codex 0.166.0's before-tool payloads (read, write, shell) after the maintainer trusted a disposable repository's hooks once in an interactive session; no bypass flag was used. Redacted, they join the Claude Code, Copilot and OpenCode fixtures, and all four normalize to the same write and shell events. Codex reads through its shell, so its read is a shell event.
- Found: Codex sends `apply_patch` text as `tool_input.command`, so the guard saw a write with no destination and denied every Codex patch. The normalizer now reads that text as the patch and keeps it away from the shell policies. The test fails without the fix.
- Decided: wholeloop still registers no native Codex hook. Codex runs a project hook only after the user trusts it, and that trust is the user's decision, so the Git fallback stays the enforced path; ADR-0049 is amended. Rejected: generating a Codex hook now, which would sit inert until trusted and look like enforcement.
- Gate: CHECK PASS in 673s, 4647 tests, macOS arm64 only; Linux not run.

## 2026-11-08 · Real hook payloads from three tier-A clients

- Captured live before-tool payloads (read, write, shell) from Claude Code 2.1.319, Copilot 1.0.83 and OpenCode 2.2.5 with recording hooks in disposable repositories, redacted paths and identifiers, and kept them as fixtures; a test shows all three normalize to the same events.
- Found: Copilot sends `apply_patch` arguments as raw patch text, which the guard rejected, so every Copilot edit through it was denied. The normalizer now reads the text as the patch.
- Live, the generated Copilot plugin's guard denies `git push` with `open-pr-publication-only` and lets an ordinary edit through. A detour moved its hook file to the plugin root on a wrong reading; bisecting showed the portable manifest's `$schema` is what makes Copilot read the vendor folder for agents and hooks, so the original layout stays.

## 2026-11-08 · Native checks on every installed client

- The read-only probe passes on Claude Code 2.1.319, Codex 0.166.0, OpenCode 2.2.5 and Copilot 1.0.83. Codex is the only one whose recipe keeps a shell; its read-only sandbox refused all three writes. The others have no write or shell tool.
- The framing scenario passes on Claude Code and OpenCode. With Codex's earlier pass, three of the four tier-A clients have a live pass; Copilot fails because headless Copilot cannot run `wl` without a shell grant, which stays a maintainer decision.
- Fixed: OpenCode 2.x takes its project from `PWD`, which every spawned worker inherited from its parent, so the first OpenCode run recorded the wholeloop checkout as its repository. Workers now get `PWD` equal to their working directory, with a test.
- Fixed in the native checks: the framing probe passed a timeout above the runner's ten-minute limit, and reported the refused command as a failed scenario; it now keeps the limit and reports any non-report output as blocked. The client never started, so those runs spent nothing.
- Blocked, each with its cause recorded in manual verification: Cursor needs workspace trust and a free-plan model, Gemini 0.67 ignores non-root system settings and needs trust (paused), and Antigravity 1.4.12 lists 60 tools for a two-tool agent, so wholeloop refuses before sending anything. Each fix widens or replaces a boundary, so each waits for the maintainer.

## 2026-11-08 · Per-client native checks

- `npm run native:check -- <client>` runs the low-quota native checks for one installed client and writes a receipt and a handoff outside the repository. Without `--live` it only observes the version; with it, a read-only probe spends one short turn and the framing scenario spends one scenario.
- The read-only probe reuses the gates' own headless recipe for that client, so it tests the boundary wholeloop actually launches. It judges the workspace, not the model: a changed workspace fails, a client that declines to try is inconclusive, and a pass needs every write refused or without a tool. Native denial signals are recorded as stronger evidence than the client's report. The framing probe drives `wl conformance` unchanged and keeps its report and redacted diagnostics.
- Tests cover the arguments, the plan per client, every read-only verdict through a fake client, the framing mapping and a handoff without fixture paths. A plan-only run against the installed Codex 0.166.0 observed its version and called no model. No task closes: each receipt is evidence for P0-03, P1-11, P1-14, P5-01 or P5-02. Hook payloads (P3-08) and MCP inheritance (P4-01) come next.

## 2026-11-08 · Philosophy: decisions in advance, individuals and teams

- [Principle 5](../docs/philosophy.md#5-people-own-every-decision-at-the-moment-or-in-advance) now reads "people own every decision, at the moment or in advance" and keeps its earlier wording visible: the supervised flow still stops at its six points by default, and autonomous delivery lets a person decide in advance through an explicit grant with pinned rubrics and limits. Some decisions are never delegated: widening the machine's authority, changing what counts as accepted and supplying a fact only a person knows. [ADR-0010](../docs/decisions/0010-interaction-profile-never-changes-coverage.md) is amended so delegation stays a grant of authority, never a profile.
- The philosophy, README and configuration pages no longer assume a team: the same questions keep one person consistent with themselves across days. A new section tells how the philosophy evolved, from one team's way of working to a repository, then to a supervised path from zero to one, then to delegated decisions and, as a direction, a configurable software factory.
- Concepts, extending and profile pages say that no configuration or profile disables a decision point and that only an explicit autonomous-delivery authorization moves one. Documentation only; docs check and Markdown lint pass.
