## 2026-11-08 · Software factory planned after 1.0.0

- A new [Phase 8](./phase-8-software-factory.md) plans unattended operation around the clock in minor releases ordered by dependency: 1.1.0 lets a run survive its own repairable failures (P6-35, P6-41 and CI continuation), 1.2.0 adds a supervisor, budgets per period and continuous intake, 1.3.0 adds evidence-based failure diagnosis, questions that park one run instead of stopping the rest, and review feedback from authorized accounts, and 1.4.0 graduates autonomous delivery to stable once it is certified. None needs a major version, because autonomous delivery stays experimental until then.
- Eight decision records are drafted as proposals 0247–0254 and left unaccepted, so they shape no 1.0.0 behaviour; documentation cannot link a proposal, so the roadmap cites them by number. Each task waits for its record. In every phase the factory never widens its own authority, never changes acceptance criteria to pass, never invents a missing fact, never declares a test flaky without repeated evidence and never retries an effect whose outcome is unknown.
- P6-35 and P6-41 move to 1.1.0. Documentation and roadmap only; docs check, Markdown lint and roadmap counts pass.

## 2026-11-08 · P1-13 ✅ · Doctor repairs that fix the failure

- Model-free preparation for the Codex trials: Codex 0.166.0 signed in on its dedicated home, quota read without a model call (95 % of the weekly window used, reset on 2026-11-12), the trial repository's protection unchanged, the six Codex boundary controls passing and the model-free EVAL-09 rehearsal on a201f79 reaching `ready-pr` with 13 stages, 14 synthetic launches, zero human waits and both environments retired in 81 s. No model call was made.
- Every failing doctor check already carried a repair command, but exercising each failure against the installed clients found two that fixed nothing: an unwritable default store suggested rerunning doctor on the same store, and an edited or deleted owned file got the instruction meant for an interrupted writer without naming the file, while install and uninstall both refuse until it is restored. Doctor now restores write access with chmod on a directory the user owns, or points at another external store, and names each changed file as edited or missing. Each repair was applied in a disposable home until doctor reported no failure.

## 2026-11-07 · P7-09 ⏸️ · Pull-request contribution workflow prepared

- The branch and tag rules are versioned files the maintainer applies: `.github/rulesets/main.json` admits changes to `main` only through squash-merged pull requests with up-to-date required checks and no bypass; `.github/rulesets/release-tags.json` lets the Release workflow create each tag once and forbids moving or deleting it. A test keeps the required checks equal to the expanded CI job names, which GitHub would otherwise only report after the rules were live.
- `.github/CODEOWNERS` assigns every path to the repository owner; the pull request template asks for a conventional title and a passing gate; the CI commit check also validates the pull request title, since a squash merge makes it the subject on `main` ([ADR-0017](../docs/decisions/0017-no-tool-attribution-in-history.md)). The contributing guide describes the workflow from the switch.
- Waiting for the maintainer: applying the rules and merge settings needs their account, and the bootstrap line in `AGENTS.md` changes only when they announce the switch, so commits still go straight to `main`. Manual verification lists the exact commands and the proposed line. Code-owner review stays off while a single maintainer cannot approve their own pull requests.
- No runtime path changed; the commit check adds one validator run per pull request. macOS full gate with two file workers: CHECK PASS in 645s, 4610/4610 tests across 328 files. Nothing was applied to the hosted repository.
- Next: P7-10, the root README for users.

## 2026-11-07 · P7-08 ✅ · Release automation

- A dispatched [release pull request workflow](../.github/workflows/release-pr.yml) sets the version, regenerates the changelog, refuses a version change smaller than the range needs and runs the full gate, then commits exactly those three files through the Git Data API (the hosted tree must equal the local one), creates the branch without overwriting, opens the pull request and dispatches CI on it, since pull requests opened with the workflow token start no checks of their own.
- The [Release workflow](../.github/workflows/release.yml) now plans from the merge: a default-branch push that changes the package version to an untagged one releases, any other push prepares nothing, and a dispatch still works. Preparation, the single `release` environment approval and byte-exact publication are unchanged; the preflight accepts that push ([ADR-0068](../docs/decisions/0068-read-only-release-consistency-preflight.md), [ADR-0069](../docs/decisions/0069-reviewed-release-bundles-and-explicit-activation.md)).
- After publication a clean Node 20 runner installs the version from the registry, checks its dist-tag and runs `wl --version` and `wl doctor`; a failure marks the GitHub Release with a caution, the run and the recovery link. The releasing guide gained the deprecate-and-supersede procedure, which stays manual because deprecation needs npm credentials no workflow holds.
- Found while extending the tests: release preparation still expected four packed files, but the package has shipped `dist/wl-runner.mjs` too since the runner was split out; the fixture had hidden it by copying only `dist/wl.mjs`. Rejected: a stored token or GitHub App so the pull request starts its own checks, Git credentials in the checkout, publishing every push, and automatic unpublishing.
- No runtime hot path changed: the new actions run only in release workflows. macOS full gate with two file workers: CHECK PASS in 663s, 4606/4606 tests across 327 files. The workflows have not run on GitHub; Linux is untested locally.
- Next: P7-09, the pull-request contribution workflow; applying the branch rules and replacing the bootstrap line need the maintainer.

## 2026-11-07 · P7-07 ✅ · Versioning policy and changelog

- Versions are full `x.y.z`, with pre-releases on `next` before a final `latest`; the releasing guide and [ADR-0068](../docs/decisions/0068-read-only-release-consistency-preflight.md) state what counts as breaking and the version change each impact needs. `release notes --from v<previous>` now checks the requested version against the range's impact and keeps an insufficient change in review, which release preparation refuses.
- `CHANGELOG.md` is generated from the same release-note drafts by `npm run changelog`, newest release first, and a new gate step fails on hand edits; a release pull request adds the untagged version with `--next`. Headings carry only the version, so that section stays byte-identical once its tag exists. No release is tagged yet, so the file says so.
- The fixture run caught git's `v:refname` order placing `1.0.0` before `1.0.0-rc.1`; tags are sorted by semantic-version precedence instead.
- No runtime hot path changed: the version rule is constant work per release-notes run and the changelog runs only in the gate and when a release is prepared. macOS full gate with two file workers: CHECK PASS in 662s, 4593/4593 tests across 326 files. Not run on Linux.

## 2026-11-07 · P7-06 ✅ · Pre-publication repository audit

- `npm run scan:history` runs gitleaks v8.30.1 from a digest-pinned image in Docker, with no network and a read-only mount, over every commit of `main`, and prints rule, file, commit and line only. Four findings were reviewed as false positives (a synthetic value in the profile privacy test and three lines of prose in ADR-0078) and listed by fingerprint with reasons in `.gitleaksignore`; the scan then reports 0 findings across 364 commits, and 4 without the file.
- Review: one author identity, no file over 1 MiB, no personal paths, only public or example addresses, no attribution trailers. Process wording, narrating how the work was carried out, was found in six commit messages and several files. With the maintainer's approval the messages were rewritten from the earliest affected commit, every later commit-hash reference in messages and files was updated in the same pass (none is stale), files were corrected separately, and `main` was force-pushed with a lease after a full gate (CHECK PASS in 669s). A local backup ref keeps the old history until it is deleted.
- macOS full gate with two file workers: CHECK PASS in 657s, 4576/4576 tests across 325 files. Not run on Linux.

- Maintainer skills and agents are authored in `maintainer/` in the canonical format and rendered by `npm run maintainer:render` into every supported client's project directories, where they are committed; [ADR-0246](../docs/decisions/0246-dogfooded-maintainer-skills-and-agents.md). ## 2026-11-07 · P6-40 ✅ · Repository guidance in autonomous runs

- `wl delivery guidance --file <proposal.json> [--answers <file>]` runs discovery and slot resolution on each repository's pinned base and proposes the plan's guidance pins with supervised precedence: one pin per resolved slot plus `repo.instructions` for AGENTS.md and its peers. Tied candidates are questions answered before authorization. [ADR-0207](../docs/decisions/0207-public-implementation-route-composition.md#repository-guidance-in-workers-p6-40) is amended; ADR-0009 and ADR-0054 note the change.
- Skill directories are pinned whole as manifests of per-file digests (32 files, 256 KiB per file, 512 KiB per pin). The rubric and templates stay single-file pins because the core reads them as bytes. The plan v2 and envelope schemas gain the optional manifest field; the unreleased v1 candidate records the new fingerprints.
- The composition reads every pin of a repository at its base with one `ls-tree` and one `cat-file --batch` instead of one `git show` per pin, refuses changed bytes, added or removed files in a pinned directory and a changed instruction set, and hands each role only its slots. Worker claims check completeness for the role; selection stays in the composition, since refusing extra context at the claim broke legitimate fixtures without adding protection.
- Narrowed: user-level skills are not candidates in autonomous runs; research-report workers receive no repository guidance; the model-backed EVAL-09 variant needs the hosted trial repository and model accounts and is listed in manual verification.
- Composition reads of pinned guidance drop from one Git process per pin to two per repository. macOS full gate with two file workers: CHECK PASS in 661s, 4519/4519 tests across 319 files. Not run on Linux.

## 2026-11-07 · P7-16 ✅ · Retire built-in service clients

- Removed the HTTP flag readers (LaunchDarkly, PostHog, GrowthBook, Unleash), the HTTP dashboard writers (Grafana, PostHog, Metabase, Mixpanel), their shared HTTP code, bridges and configuration schemas, the PostgreSQL process host and the `http` transport, with their tests. Only `mcp` and `native` remain. [ADR-0085](../docs/decisions/0085-client-owned-mcp-first-provider-transport.md) is amended with the final rule; ADR-0076–ADR-0083 are superseded and ADR-0027 and ADR-0084 note the change.
- `wl provider read-flags <id>` and `wl launch dispatch` now only plan, with `--mcp <inventory>` instead of `--bridge`/`--live`: a reviewed client MCP binding yields the exact call for the client to make; otherwise a flag state is an open question (or a local configuration read for config-flags) and a dashboard is the local pending handoff. Neither writes state or reads a credential. Their outputs moved to `wholeloop/flag-read@2` and `wholeloop/dashboard-dispatch@2` rather than changing meaning under the old identities; the unreleased v1 candidate inventory drops the six bridge schemas.
- Found and fixed while testing: the transport planner routed the virtual `wholeloop.flags.read-state` natively for providers with native access and no MCP server (LaunchDarkly, Unleash, GrowthBook), although nothing implements it locally. Such operations are now unavailable and read as unknown; those three providers need a reviewed MCP server declaration before a client binding can apply.
- New enforcer `core-no-service-network`: a test scans `src/` for network primitives and allows only the Docker application probe and proxies, the local Docker Engine socket and two loopback sandbox probes, each by file and primitive; planted connections and stale exceptions fail. The PostgreSQL read-only hard rule left with its code; the provider stays a declarative psql mapping run by the client. The two remote rollout rules now describe the client MCP path instead of the HTTP hosts.
- Docs, provider guidance and catalogue, generated command reference and the manual-verification backlog reflect the removal; the remote-bridges page is gone. No runner or store hot path changed beyond one constant-time check in transport planning.
- macOS full gate with two file workers: CHECK PASS in 669s, 4505/4505 tests across 318 files (eight bridge and dashboard test files removed, one enforcer test added), offline package, docs 481 and harness 152. Not run on Linux in this change.
- Next: P7-01, the design of the dogfooded maintenance skills and agents.

## 2026-11-07 · P7-16 · Plan the retirement of built-in service clients

- Service access was meant to go through MCP servers of the user's client or clients the user installed, not through code in this repository. The built-in HTTP flag readers (PostHog, GrowthBook, Unleash), HTTP dashboard writers (Grafana, PostHog, Metabase, Mixpanel) and an unused `psql` host remained as experimental options under ADR-0085.
- The maintainer adds [P7-16](./phase-7-release-readiness.md#p7-16--retire-built-in-service-clients) to Phase 7, before versioning, README and documentation: remove those clients and the `http` transport, keep `read-flags` and `launch dispatch` on MCP with the local handoff as fallback, delete the PostgreSQL host, and enforce that no module in `src/` opens a connection to a third-party service. The roadmap now has 107 tasks: 73 complete and 34 open.

---

## 2026-11-07 · P6-40 · Plan repository guidance in autonomous runs

- Supervised steps already resolve the target repository's skills, agents, rules and commands through `wl discover` and slot resolution. Autonomous workers deliberately run with native skill and project-instruction loading disabled and receive repository knowledge only through `plan.guidance` pins, which nothing fills from discovery; multi-file skills cannot be pinned and every pin reaches every worker.
- The maintainer adds [P6-40](./phase-6-autonomous-delivery.md#p6-40--repository-guidance-in-autonomous-runs) for 1.0.0: plan preparation proposes deterministic guidance pins from discovery, ambiguities are settled before authorization, directory pins carry per-file digests and each worker receives only its role's slots. The roadmap now has 106 tasks: 73 complete and 33 open.

---

## 2026-11-07 · Phase 7 · Plan release readiness and the public launch

- The maintainer adds [Phase 7](./phase-7-release-readiness.md) as the final phase before the first public release: maintainer skills and agents for the repository itself, performance investigation tooling, supply-chain and security automation, a pre-publication history audit, versioning and changelog, release automation, the switch to pull requests, a user-facing README, documentation passes, the public repository with a release candidate, and 1.0.0.
- Decided: maintainer tools are dogfooded (authored once in wholeloop's canonical format, rendered per client and committed, so contributors need no `wl`); publication keeps one maintainer approval; a `1.0.0-rc.1` release candidate on `next` precedes `1.0.0`, and versions are always written as full `x.y.z`; pull requests replace direct commits before the repository is public; gitleaks scans the history through Docker; the README is half story and half user guide.
- npm generates provenance only from public source repositories, even for public packages, so the visibility switch precedes the first release candidate (P7-14).
- P5-05, P5-07 and P6-32 move to Phase 7 as P7-14, P7-15 and P7-13, with their history and checked criteria unchanged; their former headings remain as redirects. The roadmap now has 105 tasks: 73 complete and 32 open.
- Next: P7-01–P7-12 do not depend on native acceptance and can start while the Phase 6 native campaign runs.

## 2026-11-07 · P6-39 ✅ · Close the performance audit at the measured state

- The maintainer withdraws the initial five-second controller ceiling, set before its consequences were known, and closes P6-39 at the measured state; performance is no longer a 1.0 release blocker. [ADR-0227](../docs/decisions/0227-verified-read-observation-scopes.md) is amended; the matching criterion of P6-27 is withdrawn.
- Measured state: the complete model-free EVAL-09 route takes 68–72 seconds (about 175 at the start of the audit); the controller takes 60.3 seconds, about 20 of them waiting for supervised work, so wholeloop overhead is about 40 seconds, mostly guard Git processes, committed-fact flushes and closure proofs. Reaching five seconds would have meant removing guard points or durability.
- Narrowed rather than met: per-path traces of every canonical step and entry point beyond the complete route, the missing-input step traces and the component controls. Untraced paths are unaudited, not claimed fast; a future performance problem is an ordinary defect. No complete-route counter budget is added.
- Remaining 1.0 work: P6-27 native evaluations and P6-35 worker credentials, four implemented tasks awaiting native or hosted acceptance, and the fourteen original native, platform and release tasks.

## 2026-11-07 · P6-39 · Borrowed views, joined queries and directory durability

- Internal readers borrow frozen journal projections, resolved store files are read with one canonicalization, the review audit and patch are shared within one decision and clean credential scans are remembered by content: the calibrated controller goes from 67.945 to 63.882 seconds.
- Durability correction: a fact written into a directory the store had just created (for example each native invocation's intent record) could vanish with that directory after power loss, because only the file and its own directory were flushed. Every directory the store creates now has its entry flushed in its parent ([ADR-0242](../docs/decisions/0242-flush-only-committed-facts.md)); this adds about 320 flushes to the route and closes the gap listed in [execution isolation](../docs/architecture/execution-isolation.md).
- HEAD, branch and Git directory are asked in joined `rev-parse` queries outside sealed observations; a shared `DecisionMemo` replaces two copies of the per-decision rule; path containment of normalized absolute paths skips `path.relative`; installation backups flush their directory once per batch ([ADR-0243](../docs/decisions/0243-single-writer-store-trust-model.md), [ADR-0242](../docs/decisions/0242-flush-only-committed-facts.md)). Final calibrated route: controller 60.279 seconds, 3388 Git processes (3674 before), 2919 flushes; uninstrumented rehearsals 68 and 72 seconds.
- Rejected after measurement: joined queries inside sealed observations (they lose sharing by key) and a per-decision memo of the local Git configuration check (it missed a helper configured between two back-to-back actions).
- macOS full gate: CHECK PASS in 650s, 4877/4877 tests across 325 files with two file workers. Offline Linux gate on the exact snapshot (1832 files; runtime e56f7ca207e394dbf3adcdab539875d2def14118f62767479223909be20fbd77): CHECK PASS in 157s, 4875 passes and the same two environment skips; the root-skipped file passes 7/7 as uid 65534; Linux-built bundles match macOS.
- P6-39 stays open. Remaining controller time is synchronous Git and `ps` at guard points, committed-fact flushes and waiting on supervised work; details in the [audit ledger](./performance-audit.md).

## 2026-11-05 · P6-38 · Output failure regression — in progress

- Reproduce a distinct output failure deterministically: a synchronous EPIPE from the sink was treated as an unreadable store, attempted a second frame on the broken output and exited 1. A synchronous EIO similarly retried the sink. Cancel on caught output errors as well as emitted stdout errors, retain the first code only, and leave unexpected I/O failures nonzero. No raw error text enters a frame. Both regressors fail before the fix and pass after it; no extra platform error code is guessed from the earlier unretained failure.

The complete focused overview/watch suite passes 170 tests in fifteen files with the concurrent-reader and output fixes. Type checking and lint pass. ## 2026-11-04 · P6-27 · Worker credential design drafted — in progress

- Drafted `docs/decisions/0209-controller-owned-native-credentials.md` (Proposed). The controller would keep every worker credential in the OS secret store and inject it per launch. Workers would have disposable homes and no credential store access, there would be a preflight before any claim, and an exhausted usage window would pause the run. Antigravity is documented as an unavailable worker, while its in-session harness use is unaffected.
- The draft was written only after probing all seven installed clients with fabricated credentials and empty homes (zero tokens). Injection works on Claude Code (API key and subscription token, on macOS and Linux and inside the profile with the Keychain denied), Codex (`CODEX_API_KEY`; ChatGPT sign-in refreshes itself), Gemini, OpenCode, Copilot and Cursor. Antigravity 1.4.7 requires a browser sign-in in `-p` mode. Those probes opened sign-in tabs on the host's screen until the later probes blocked browser launches; the probes' leftover OpenCode services were stopped.
- Acceptance is decided after real calls per client.

## 2026-11-04 · P6-27 · Three-run rule and 1.0 client scope — in progress

- The three-run rule was set. An attempt the retained receipt shows was refused by the observer, or denied for credentials or its usage window, is an infrastructure failure: it is repeated without invalidating its batch and stays reported. Taken in order, the first three remaining attempts must all pass. `eval:campaign` applied a laxer rule (any three passes) before; it now follows this one. Claude EVAL-10 stays `three-passes`: six infrastructure failures are set aside and the four remaining attempts all passed.
- The 1.0 scope was confirmed: Claude Code and Codex are the autonomous-delivery reference clients. Gemini, OpenCode, Copilot and Cursor follow as workers in 1.x through a shared client kit, and their harness support stays in 1.0. Antigravity stays unavailable as a worker until it has a headless credential path. Credential probes on all seven clients support this: injection works on six, and Antigravity requires browser sign-in in `-p` mode.

- After a fresh sign-in, three EVAL-10 trials on code 6809993 gave one pass and two failures. Trial 1 completed with outcome `completed-with-unknowns`: 867 s, no human wait, 29 launches, both delegated decisions accepted and supported, twelve cited claims, no refutation, four open questions and 58,123 output tokens. Trial 2 passed its plan, then its research launches exited `permission-denied` and the route ended blocked after 721 s. Trial 3 was blocked within 3 s with zero tokens.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-27 · Claude trials stopped by a token refresh — in progress

- Three EVAL-10 trials on Claude Code 2.1.316 were approved (code 183b4a0). None reached the model. The first was admitted, exited `permission-denied` with zero tokens, and the client wrote `.credentials.json` into its client home; the observer refused the other two for that file. The likely cause, unverified, is a token refresh that could not write the read-only Keychain inside the profile.
- The profile now denies creating `.credentials.json`, `credentials.json` and `auth.json` in the client home, so a refresh that cannot reach the Keychain fails without a plaintext credential. A real `sandbox-exec` case covers it, and removing the deny fails it. How tokens are refreshed during long campaigns is a maintainer decision; until then the client home is signed in again before each batch.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-29 · Public continuation run — in progress

- The public `to-pr` composition registers the correction adapter, so a continuation route is covered as a whole (ADR-0208, increment 4).
- New `eval:public-continuation` uses the real engine, real Docker QA and real Git with a synthetic client behind the real Codex observer. The first run's product QA fails. The operator retires that run's environments, records the correction revision and grants it. The second run seeds the unit at the failed head, rebuilds, corrects from the retained failure and completes `ready-pr` with the corrected bytes published.
- It found that the failed-case reader required the current basis (it now checks the original binding against the record's own basis), and that a failed run's live environments block the continuation's capacity. Retiring them stays an explicit operator action.
- The development loop now has `npm run check:fast` per commit and the full `npm run check` before any push and at task close, both ending in one `CHECK PASS` or `CHECK FAIL (<step>)` line. This change of working rule was approved the same day.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-29 · Correction stage — in progress

- The fixed dispatcher registers `RunnerCorrectionStages` (ADR-0208, increment 3). The stage reads the predecessor's retained failure, including failed product cases for an integration origin, with private evaluator cases reduced to their id and status. It then fences and requeues the unit's builder with a correction task and has the controller commit the result. The unit's candidate resolves to that commit for every QA stage.
- Writing it exposed a queue rule that blocked every continuation: a settled attempt of an earlier basis kept its slot closed, and the journal cannot fence across bases. Slot admission now counts only current-basis attempts. A continuation only reopens with nothing unresolved.
- A hybrid case with a real builder and real Git runs build, failed integration, continuation and correction; QA reads the corrected bytes. Removing the candidate fallback or the queue change fails it.
- Not covered yet: a dedicated test that private cases are hidden, richer acceptance-origin feedback, and verification repairs on top of a correction, which block honestly.
- Next: public composition and a synthetic continuation run.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-29 · Seeded workspaces in continuations — in progress

- A continuation no longer reuses workspace, commit or acceptance-definition stages: their records are bound to the earlier grant or revision. Every stage depends on them, so an implementation continuation runs its whole route again. The ADR-0197 cases now assert that (ADR-0208, increment 2).
- In a continuation the Git workspace stage seeds the unit's new workspace from the predecessor request's own commit stage. A seeded unit whose builder changes nothing commits with `--allow-empty`, so it keeps an owned commit; normal workspaces are unchanged.
- A hybrid run (real Git stages, synthetic everything else) fails integration, continues under a new grant and starts the new topic at exactly the failed head with an empty owned commit on top. It also found that the creation observer expected the declared base, so every seeded creation was `binding-mismatch`; increment 1's case now checks the observed status too. Removing either fix fails the cases.
- Next: the `correction` stage adapter.

---

## 2026-11-03 · P6-29 · Seeded correction workspaces — in progress

- ADR-0208 designs the correction producer for `none` policies. ADR-0197 gave units other than the corrected one a seeded candidate that skips implementation, but under `none` the build plan's delegated decision is never carried into a new revision, so it and the builder run again. Git records are bound to their grant, so no workspace or commit stage can be reused, and the acceptance plan belongs to one revision. Every unit is therefore seeded at its committed head and its chain runs again under the new grant; no decision rule is relaxed.
- First increment: a workspace request may name a predecessor commit record as its seed. The head is read as history from the old topic's latest applied, host-observed commit and must be a commit object descending from the declared base. Only independent units are seeded. The topic branch starts there. The request, record and status contracts gain the optional `seed`; their fingerprints are updated.
- Four cases: the seeded topic starts at the old head on a new branch, and an unknown record, a plan with no previous revision and a seeded unit with predecessor parts are refused before any effect.
- Next: reuse exclusions and seeded workspace stages in a continuation.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-27 · Complete synthetic to-pr run — in progress

- `eval:public-implementation` now hands the request to the real engine (ADR-0207, increment 7). The run ends `completed` with outcome `ready-pr`: thirteen host-observed stages, fourteen launches each preceded by its controls, and no wait for input. It used real Docker unit and product QA and a real Git push, without force, to a local bare repository behind an SSH-form GitHub remote. One owned pull request was opened as a draft and readied by a synthetic `gh`, and both environments were retired.
- That run found that no to-pr run could ever complete. Publication returns the run to `queued` after its own effect, and completion was only allowed from `running` or `waiting-external`. Completion from `queued` is now allowed only with an applied effect on the current basis; every other guard stays. A journal case covers both sides.
- Failed debugging runs kept their Docker resources by design and used up Docker's address pools. They were removed by their exact fixture-run label after checking that nothing else carried it.
- Next: P6-29's seeded correction producer.

- Publication and the `to-pr` handoff are composed with the fixed `GithubCliCodeHost`, whose executor runs the runtime's absolute `gh` (ADR-0207, increment 6). The whole route is now covered and passes static preflight; a runtime without a code host composes nothing. A unit case checks the executor's exact `gh` arguments against a synthetic binary.
- Publication itself has not run: `git remote get-url` expands `insteadOf`, so a local remote cannot sit behind a GitHub URL. The complete synthetic run will use an SSH-form remote served from a local bare repository through `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-27 · Public to-pr integration and acceptance — in progress

- Integration and acceptance are composed (ADR-0207, increment 5). Each milestone gets a deterministic product production: per-repository verify and review policies pinned as store files, the shared runtime recipe and integration-owned repairs. A product production may now take `acceptance: "pinned"`, which resolves the plan pinned for the revision when the milestone is produced, because a host is composed before its acceptance definition runs.
- The real run found that the acceptance stage required every call in its native scope to finish with exit zero. Real observers add an owned cancellation control to each launch, stopped with SIGTERM by design. A cancelled call within the original window no longer fails acceptance. A finished call must still exit zero, and a cancelled judge still leaves its decision unready.
- `eval:public-implementation` passes all eleven stages from plan to acceptance with real Docker unit and product QA, fourteen launches and both environments retired. The existing `eval:runner-assembly-readiness` still passes. Four new validation cases cover the production and composition refusals.
- Next: publication through the runtime's `gh`.

## 2026-11-03 · P6-27 · Public to-pr gates — in progress

- Verification, review and re-verification are composed (ADR-0207, increment 4). Gates launch read only through the fixed observer built for the unit workspace. Each gate's flow engine is configured only from a store-owned file: `verify.suite` is bound to the runtime's suite and `review.rubric` to the plan's pinned rubric. Its home is an empty store directory, so the user's own configuration is never read.
- Real Docker showed that a suite ref names an acceptance case, and a revision has exactly one acceptance plan, pinned before builders. The acceptance-definition stage now appends controller-owned `cli` cases derived from the pinned recipe, and a produced case may not redefine one. The ADR's wording on the mapping is corrected in place.
- New `npm run eval:public-implementation`: a synthetic client behind the real Codex observer, with real Docker unit QA, passes all nine stages from plan to re-verification. There were twelve launches, each preceded by its controls, and one environment, which was retired. The first run found a case timeout set to the command's 5 s, which cancelled the case during container setup.

- One model turn inside the launch profile was approved. It found that Claude Code ignores `TMPDIR` and creates `/tmp/claude-<uid>`, which the profile denies and other sessions share. - ADR-0205. The build now emits a self-contained `dist/wl-runner.mjs` and embeds its SHA-256 in the core. `wl delivery run` launches the verified bundle as the owned child and refuses a missing or changed one before claiming the run. The core is 3.8 MB, under the 4 MiB copy bound, and both files ship. The process boundary keeps one copy of each class, which a dynamic import into the core would not.

- Found while preparing the public host: a native research worker is told to query its source angle, but every recipe removed web tools. ## 2026-11-03 · P6-27 · Contained Claude Code boundary — in progress

- Claude Code had no boundary observer, so no Claude stage could be admitted. Two observations on 2.1.316 shaped the fix. First, the client reads its login from the macOS Keychain, which needs the real home and `USER`, so the per-assignment home made every launch unauthenticated; this had gone unseen because only synthetic clients ran. Second, the client has no policy-only probe.
- The fix is ADR-0203. A Claude launch runs inside a `sandbox-exec` read/write allow-list profile carried in argv, and therefore in the policy digest. The client home is dedicated and was signed in once by hand. `ClaudeBoundaryHost` runs the controller's probe, cancellation and the client's own login status under that profile without a model. It is macOS only; off macOS Claude stays unavailable. The workspace-witness helper moved to `native-workspace.ts` for both observers.
- Ten unit cases, plus a launch case showing the sandboxed command and environment. A mispaired client home is now refused before an attempt is prepared; the first version registered the attempt and then threw. Six real controls pass on the installed client, and the eval refuses once the profile's leading deny is removed. A model turn under the profile has not run; it needs approval to spend quota.
- Next: public host composition.

## 2026-11-02 · P6-30 · Learning observation host — complete

- Register `RunnerObservationStages` for the observation stage and the `learning-observation` handoff (ADR-0201). Reads go through the client's MCP connection via `clientProviderHost`, never an HTTP bridge, at most 64 `source.*.query` reads, each needing a grant read rule scoped to the observed subject. Unserved or refused reads stay unknown. Readings are stored before `ObservationSchedule.settle` records the outcome once, now naming the run and invocation in `by`; an interrupted read settles nothing. The native judge and challenger decide `learning-outcome` over the exact brief through the fixed decision stages with an empty owned prefix. The handoff stores the effect-free recommendation as a run artifact. Supervised observation routes are refused at static preflight (conservative refinement, as for report research).
- Bug found while testing: a recommendation's proposal field was `authorization`, which the run's credential scan correctly refuses as an `authorization: <token>` header, so every `iterate`, `kill` and `unflag` recommendation was unstorable. It is now `authority`; the record was never a published contract.
- Eight engine-driven cases cover a complete none-policy run, unknowns under both contracts, an ungranted read, a `kill` proposal, an interrupted read and the supervised refusal. A scheduler replay runs the built `wl measure tick` twice as separate processes at fixed times and then completes the run. Assumption: replaying the firings is the "actual local scheduler replay"; no cron or launchd daemon was installed. No real MCP server, provider or model was used. P6-30 is ✅; the roadmap is 68/88.

## 2026-11-02 · P6-24 · Composed conditional merge routes — awaiting hosted trials

- Compose `to-merge` routes as `publication`, `ci`, `merge` and `handoff` (ADR-0199). The operator's merge policy (method, trusted required checks, denylist and exceptions) is a new optional `plan.policy.merge`. It is required for `to-merge` and refused elsewhere, and every grant binds it. The CI stage waits finitely for revision-bound checks at the published head and keeps a typed repair request on failure. The merge stage recomputes eligibility immediately before each single expected-head merge from: this request's gates; a fresh CI read; and complete pull-request facts from the new `GithubCliMergeFactsHost`, where each unreadable fact is unknown. Handoff and the `merged` endpoint accept only the run's own exact-head merge.
- Conservative choices recorded in ADR-0199: the dispatcher refuses a merge route at static preflight unless the installed merge stages carry an explicit runtime allowance; the public `wl delivery run` host still composes no publication or merge stages; re-verification counts as refutation in the eligibility evidence.
- Contract changes are additive on the unreleased v1 candidate: `policy.merge` in the plan, envelope, grant and their outputs, and the `ci` and `merge` stage kinds. Fingerprints are updated with that reason. Existing tests that relied on the old refusal now check the runtime refusal and the new route order.
- Eleven dispatcher-composed stub cases and four stub-executor cases. A mutation that forces eligibility fails all seven refusal cases. P6-24 is ⏸️ awaiting the separately authorized hosted protection and exact-SHA trials (REL-10, EVAL-05, EVAL-08).

## 2026-11-02 · P6-29 · Correction producer design — deferred to P6-27

- Owned workspaces and commit receipts are valid evidence only under the run's current grant and basis; `validateOwnedWorkspaceCommit` enforces this even in its historical mode. Because every continuation needs a new exact-plan grant, owned resources from the failed run cannot be current evidence for it. A first attempt to read them across requests failed on exactly that invariant and was discarded without a commit.
- Of three options, the design that keeps the invariant was chosen. The corrected unit gets a workspace seeded at its failed head under the new grant; every other unit feeding a stage that runs again gets a seeded candidate at its existing head and is re-verified; original stages are history only. Relaxing the invariant for an older grant of the same run was rejected.
- Because the producer needs a seeded-candidate notion across every commit-receipt consumer and can only be exercised for real by native clients, it was deferred to P6-27. The design is recorded in ADR-0197 and the phase file. P6-29 stays 🔄 and P6-22's second criterion stays open. Next: P6-24.

## 2026-11-02 · P6-29 · Correction revisions and stage reuse — in progress

- Implement the continuation of ADR-0193 as a correction revision (ADR-0197). `wl delivery continue` previews, and records only when accepted, the next plan revision with a `corrections` entry: owning unit, origin, predecessor request, failed stage and exact failure digest. The reducer reopens a `failed` or `blocked` run only for that retained integration or acceptance failure, within the journal-derived `integrationRepairs` budget, after every stage, attempt and effect is resolved. The route adds `correction-N:<unit>` before the unit's verification. The controller reuses a predecessor stage only when the revision impact keeps it, it needs no delegated decision, its predecessors were reused and the host re-inspects the original with the same evidence; everything else runs again. The report lists the continue action and the chain.
- Authority choice: every continuation needs a new exact-plan grant, and the journal refuses the failed run's grant and any envelope grant. The wider option for unattended `none` runs is a Proposed draft, `docs/decisions/0198-envelope-grant-continuation.md`, with a row under maintainer actions in manual verification.
- Contract changes are additive on the unreleased v1 candidate (ADR-0066): optional `corrections` in the plan, `continues` in registration, the `reused` runner action and `origin`, runner-facet `continuations`, the `correction` stage kind, report `continuations` and one new output, `wholeloop/delivery-continue@1`. Their fingerprints are updated with that reason.

## 2026-11-01 · Decisions for P6-10 recorded

- Trusted native bindings: option 1 was accepted, so ADR-0180 is Accepted and indexed. No native client is trusted unless explicitly listed, and a listing can never loosen containment. Proving real native clients is P6-27's job; P6-10's public run and reconcile commands must work with the empty default and with explicit bindings, tested with the synthetic/reference transport.
- Supervised derived plans: the operator issues a fresh exact-plan grant for each envelope-derived plan; a person's recorded approval never becomes authority for later steps. Envelope-derived supervised routes and supervised definition approvals stay refused before dispatch. Recorded as ADR-0182, a new record because the repository refines accepted ADRs with new ones rather than amending them.
- Repair continuation moves to P6-29's deliverables, with its own ADR for the cross-invocation correction protocol that ADR-0154 records as missing; until then a failed integration or acceptance stage ends the run honestly.
- P6-10 closure: hosted endpoints (P6-20–P6-24) and report-level research (P6-33) are not closure conditions. P6-10 closes when its three acceptance criteria hold with automated evidence (compiled CLI lifecycle, process-group cancellation and interactive-tool canaries) using the synthetic/reference transport. That evidence certifies neither a native client nor a hosted service.
- Add the standing rule to AGENTS.md under "How to work a session": choose the most conservative option for implementation refinements and continue; stop only for widened authority, hosted or publication effects, or signed-in native clients, and even then finish everything testable with fixtures. Remove the three answered maintainer actions from manual verification.
- Markdown lint passes on 411 files. The complete check passes 3545 tests in 188 files, lint 1022, types, build, smoke, offline packaging, docs 412, generators, harness 151 and Mermaid 36 in 553 seconds.

## 2026-11-01 · P6-10 decision proposed — trusted native bindings

- Write the trusted native bindings decision as a Proposed record, `docs/decisions/0180-trusted-native-bindings.md`, with the most conservative default: trust no native client unless explicitly bound and never relax containment. - Add typed items, notes, links and readable projections under the external XDG store, rejecting repositories, traversal and symlink escapes before writes.
- Use one lock and atomic monthly journal replacement per validated transaction. Reads replay the journal so projection recovery remains deterministic after interruption; ADR-0020 records why stale locks are not stolen.
- Verify replay, batch rollback, corruption handling, lock contention and twelve concurrent real writer processes alongside the complete local check suite. Physical process-interruption checks remain manual verification.

---

## 2026-10-18 · P1-02 — deterministic layered configuration

- Define the strict public schema and deterministic user/project/initiative/run loader, with source explanations, personal/team precedence, per-key map merging and array replacement.
- Reject unknown keys, duplicate YAML mappings and missing explicit layer files. Expand preset defaults in each layer before resolving precedence.
- Generate the configuration reference from the same schema; verify the full local check suite including precedence and malformed configuration fixtures.

---

## 2026-10-18 · P0-06 — commit hygiene enforcement

- Enforce Conventional Commit subjects, explanatory bodies and the no-tool-attribution rule through one validator shared by the CLI, optional local hook and CI range check.
- Keep hook installation explicit and preserve an existing custom hook path. CI resolves the actual pull-request base/head and never evaluates message content as shell code.
- Verified the full local check suite, real hook execution in an isolated repository, malformed messages and pull-request range selection.

## 2026-10-18 · P1-01 — versioned command framework

- Add one command registry, strict option parsing, versioned JSON for help/version and every implemented command, and contained error formatting with all five documented exit codes.
- Generate the implemented command reference from definitions and check it for drift, keeping the target flow surface explicitly planned.
- Verified the complete local check suite, including command/error contracts, documentation generation, build and smoke on macOS arm64.

## 2026-10-14 · P0-02 done — CI green

### Done

- First CI run failed only on Node 20: `jsdom` 30 (Mermaid check) needs Node 22 APIs. CI now has a `check` job (Ubuntu + macOS, Node 22, full `npm run check`) and a `runtime` job that builds on 22 and runs the bundled CLI on Node 20. All green.
- Development requires Node ≥ 22; the published CLI supports Node ≥ 20 ([ADR-0019](../docs/decisions/0019-typescript-toolchain.md)).
- `origin` switched to SSH (the machine has no HTTPS credential helper; the SSH key authenticates as `dreamingechoes`).

### Open

- Agent sessions cannot push or edit `.github/workflows/` on this machine; the maintainer applies those.

---

## 2026-10-14 · P0-04 and P0-05 done — docs and harness checks

### Done

- `wl docs nav` regenerates every footer from `docs/nav.yml`; `wl docs check` verifies docnav drift, nav listing, status banners, relative links and anchors (GitHub slugs) across 71 Markdown files.
- `npm run lint:mermaid` parses all 35 diagrams with the official Mermaid parser (dev-only; too heavy for the bundled CLI).
- `wl harness check` enforces size budgets, portable skill and agent front matter, and `enforcement.yml` (missing enforcer · prose as enforcer · missing file or symbol · `forbid` contradictions · stale entries · documentation-only without reason).
- `npm run check` now runs lint · typecheck · 51 unit tests · build · smoke · docs check · harness check · markdownlint · Mermaid in about 4 seconds; CI runs the same command.

### Found by the new checks

- `docs/nav.yml` had an unquoted title with commas that YAML silently split; `parseNav` now rejects unknown keys.
- Glossary and FAQ had no status banner; added.

### Decided

- Bundled dependencies stay in `devDependencies`: `dist/wl.mjs` is self-contained, so the published package has no runtime dependencies.
- npm's optional-dependency bug left the lockfile without platform bindings for Vitest's bundler; the lockfile was regenerated from scratch. If `vitest` fails with a missing `rolldown` binding, regenerate the lockfile rather than adding the binding as a dependency.

## 2026-10-14 · P0-02 in progress — TypeScript toolchain

### Done

- `package.json` (npm, Node ≥ 20, exact dev dependencies published ≥ 2 weeks earlier), strict `tsconfig`, Biome (recommended preset), Vitest, esbuild bundle to `dist/wl.mjs` with the version injected from `package.json`.
- Minimal `wl`: `--version`, `--help`, unknown input → stderr + exit 1; 6 unit tests; `npm run smoke` checks the built binary.
- `npm run check` (lint · typecheck · tests · markdownlint) runs in about 2 seconds.
- CI workflow: Ubuntu (Node 20, 22) and macOS (Node 22), actions pinned by SHA, runtime-dependency audit.
- markdownlint upgraded to 0.23.3; its new table rule (MD060) required normalising every table delimiter row.
- Decisions in [ADR-0019](../docs/decisions/0019-typescript-toolchain.md): npm over pnpm, Biome over ESLint + Prettier, TypeScript 7.

### Decided

- Patched transitive versions forced through `overrides` (`smol-toml` 1.9.0, `katex` 0.18.9).
- `braces` ≤ 3.0.3 advisory (dev-only, no fix released) accepted and documented; it never sees untrusted input.
- Biome's `migrate` silently turned the recommended rules off (`preset: none`); fixed to `preset: recommended` and verified with a probe file. Worth remembering on future Biome upgrades.

### Open

- **CI green on Linux and macOS** cannot be verified until these commits are pushed (pushes from agent sessions are currently blocked on this machine — see the P0-01 entry).

## 2026-10-14 · P0-01 in progress — identity and repository

### Done

- `throughline` turned out to be crowded by active tools for the same CLIs; the project is now **wholeloop**, CLI alias `wl` ([ADR-0018](../docs/decisions/0018-project-name.md)). Docs, ADRs and roadmap renamed in one commit; local folder renamed.
- Private repository created at `github.com/dreamingechoes/wholeloop`; `main` pushed with full history at creation.
- Issue forms (bug, idea, provider request), PR template, `SECURITY.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` (Contributor Covenant 2.1).

### Open

- **npm reservation** — `wholeloop` is free on the registry, but nobody is logged in to npm on this machine, and publishing a placeholder makes the name public. Waiting on the maintainer: `npm login`, then approval to publish `0.0.0`.
- **Pushing from agent sessions** — a global push guard from another plugin on the development machine blocks `git push`; the maintainer pushes, or excludes this repository from that guard.
- GitHub private vulnerability reporting is unavailable while the repository is private; `SECURITY.md` points to email until then.

### Next

- Close P0-01 once the npm name is reserved. P0-02 and P0-03 do not depend on it and can start.

## 2026-10-14 · Repository bootstrapped

### Done

- Repository created: license, editor settings, README with the philosophy and the flow.
- Documentation skeleton: philosophy, concepts, the flow and every step page, configuration (profile, providers, repo skills), extending, platforms, architecture, contributing guides, glossary, FAQ.
- Foundational decisions recorded as ADR-0001 to ADR-0018.
- Roadmap: six phases, 54 tasks with acceptance criteria.

### Decided

- Name `wholeloop` (CLI `wl`), chosen after `throughline` turned out to be crowded by active tools for the same CLIs ([ADR-0018](../docs/decisions/0018-project-name.md)); TypeScript core; MIT; personal open-source project.
- State outside every repository; builds in git worktrees so slices run in parallel.
- Tier A: Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Copilot CLI. Tier B: Gemini CLI, Antigravity, Cursor.
- Every step usable on its own; the full flow is opt-in through `next`.
- Providers and other extensions are declarative manifests over a capability taxonomy with no dangerous verbs.
- Gate round budgets default to verify 4 / review 3 and are configurable per project and per run.
- No tool attribution anywhere in the history.
- Repository hosted privately at `github.com/dreamingechoes/wholeloop` (personal account).
- Bootstrap workflow: commits go straight to `main`, no branches or PRs, until the maintainer announces the switch.

### Next

- [P0-01](./phase-0-foundations.md#p0-01--names-package-and-repository-hygiene) — reserve the package name and push the repository.
- [P0-03](./phase-0-foundations.md#p0-03--cli-conformance-spike) can start in parallel: it needs no code from this repo.
